## 0 · The year in one paragraph

Marat and Yuliana file jointly with **two dependent children**. 2025 is a much busier year than
2024. Marat runs **Marat Boxing LLC** on a Schedule C (his second year) and picked up **two
partnership K-1s that did not exist in 2024** — a loss from each. Yuliana has **a Schedule C for
the first time** (two 1099-NECs), a **home office for the first time**, her **K-1 from Gossip
Miami**, and she **sold her half of Gossip Miami on 30 October 2025**, which lands on her Schedule D
as a **long-term capital LOSS**. The household stays on **Marketplace health insurance**, which in
2024 produced almost $18,300 of advance credit and is the single largest cash item on the return.

**The shape of the answer:** taxable income is again **zero** (AGI **25,083** against a 31,500
standard deduction), so there is **no regular income tax**; the tax that is actually due is
**self-employment tax**, and the refund is driven by the **refundable child credit and the EIC**.
🔴 **AND THAT LAST CLAUSE IS WHY THE DRAFT IN ATX CURRENTLY SHOWS 5,003 DUE RATHER THAN A REFUND:**
the Dependents block on page 1 is empty and Form 8962 is not in the return, so **both** of the things
that make this return a refund are absent. **§3L is the list, in the order to fix it.**

Act disclosure box** — on 2026-09-11 *("es doc es un lease agreement")*, which is source row **16**
and is what §3F is built on. ✅ **§6 item 27 is CLOSED on that reading** — the question it asked was
*lease or financed purchase*, and it is answered. ⛔ **But the other ten pages are still unread**, and
what is on them is not nothing: **the mileage allowance, the excess-mileage rate and the turn-in fee
are known; the wear-and-tear terms, any guarantor, and whose name is on the lease are not.**
🛠️ **Whose name is on the lease rides with §6 item 29's client message** — it is the only one of them
a person can answer without going back to the document.
ⓘ **Housekeeping, recorded because limit 6 and the delete obligation are what a later reader checks:** both raw
copies were fetched to the **session scratchpad** *(never the working tree)*, hashed, and **deleted in the same
step**; the redactor itself wrote nothing, because it fails closed before writing.

### ⚠️ What the redactor reported — and the two places it could not see

| Document | Result |
|---|---|
| The three `.xlsx` | ✅ clean — **0 SSN, 0 bank/account**; one EIN kept (Marat Boxing) |
| PEP Hollywood K-1 package | 8 pages; **5 SSN/ITIN and 4 street lines masked**, 5 EINs kept. ⚠️ **page 1 barely extracted** |
| Development Strategies K-1 | 3 pages; 3 SSN/ITIN, 2 street lines masked |
| The three 1099-NECs | clean; payer EINs kept |
| Organizer | 6 pages; 9 long digit-runs masked. ⚠️ **page 6 barely extracted** |
| 2024 return | 38 pages; **1 account/routing, 2 long digit-runs, 5 street lines** masked, 6 EINs kept. ⚠️ **pages 1–5 barely extracted** *(they are the cover/instruction sheets — pages 6 onward carry every form)* |
| 🔴 **`33806941-Contract.pdf`** — the car contract | ⛔ **Exit 2 · NO TEXT LAYER · 11 pages. Nothing extracted, nothing masked, nothing written** — it is a scan, and the tool fails closed before writing. **Not worked around** *(2026-09-11, on Lilian's authorisation)* |
| 🔴 **2025 Form 1095-A** | **8 pages; only 1–3 came through.** A retry in pypdf's `layout` mode gave page 4 = *"This page is intentionally left blank"* and **pages 5–8 = 0, 0, 9 and 170 characters.** ⛔ **An absence on those pages is NOT evidence — the tables are there and this tool cannot read them** |

> 🛠️ **A GAP IN THE FIRM'S OWN TOOLING, RAISED NOT FIXED.** `redact.py` reads **PDF only**, and three
> of this return's four source documents are `.xlsx`. For this session the workbooks were flattened
> to text and pushed through **the redactor's own `normalise()` / `redact()` and a fail-closed
> guard**, in a scratchpad wrapper — so the never-print-the-text property held. ⛔ **A safety tool
> was not edited in the middle of a return.** Adding `.xlsx` support to `tools/redact-doc/` properly,
> with a test, is [`FOLLOW-UPS.md`](../../../FOLLOW-UPS.md).
>
> 🔴 **AND A REAL REDACTOR MISS, found on the 2024 return.** The bank **routing number printed with a
> space between every digit** on Form 1040 line 35b came through **unmasked** — the patterns expect
> `NNNNNNNNN` or grouped separators, not `N N N N N N N N N`. The account number was masked by the
> form itself, not by the tool. ⛔ **Nothing was written anywhere**, but the control did not work.
> Also in `FOLLOW-UPS.md`.

## 2 · 🛑 What the IRS changed for 2025 — read off the current PDFs, not from memory

> ⓘ **Two section numbers depart from [`_workpaper-template.md`](../_workpaper-template.md), so a reader who knows the template is not left hunting.** The template's **§2 *Adjusting entries made before preparing*** does not arise — the firm keeps no books for this client and made no entries; every adjustment is a line-level correction and sits with its own line in §3. The template's **§5 *Tie-out checks*** is **§3K *The return adds up***, where the whole chain from the two Schedule Cs to the refund is reconciled in one table; §5 here is the sensitivity analysis instead.

**Every line address in this paper was read off the 2025 form downloaded from irs.gov on
2026-09-10.** Four things moved, and three of them would produce a wrong return if keyed from last
year's numbering.

| Form | 2024 | **2025** |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **Schedule C** | **27a** Other expenses · **27b** Form 7205 | ⚠️ **THEY SWAPPED: 27a is the Form 7205 energy deduction, 27b is `Other expenses (from line 48)`** |
| 🔴 **Form 1040** | line **7** capital gain · line **11** AGI | **7a** capital gain (7b is a checkbox pair) · **11a** AGI on page 1, **11b** repeated on page 2 |
| 🔴 **Form 1040** | line 12 standard deduction · 13 QBI | **12e** standard/itemised · **13a** QBI · 🆕 **13b `Additional deductions from Schedule 1-A, line 38`** · 14 = 12e+13a+13b · 15 taxable income |
| 🆕 **Schedule 1-A** | did not exist | Four new deductions: **qualified tips** (l.4–13) · **qualified overtime** (l.14–21) · **passenger-vehicle loan interest** (l.22–30, needs the **VIN**) · **enhanced senior deduction** (l.31–37). §3K |
| **Schedule 8812** | 2,000/child · ACTC 1,700 | **$2,200 per child** (line 5) · **ACTC still ×$1,700** (line 16b) · threshold **$400,000 MFJ** (line 9) |
| **Standard deduction MFJ** | 29,200 | **31,500** *(read off the 2025 Form 1040 margin)* |
| **Form 8962 line 4, family of 4, 48 states** | 30,000 | **31,200** *(2025 Instructions Table 1-1 — for 2025 coverage the **2024** poverty lines are used)* |
| **Form 7203** | Rev. 12-2022 | **still Rev. 12-2022** — no change |
| **Schedule E page 2** | unchanged | unchanged: **line 28 is identity only**, the money grid sits below it with the same row letters |

## 3 · The forms, line by line

### 🧭 The order of preparation — and the one circularity

```
Marat's Schedule C  (all of Part II)  ──────────────► line 31
Yuliana's Schedule C, everything EXCEPT line 30
        │  line 29
        ▼
   Form 8829  (line 8 ← Sch C line 29)
        │  line 36
        ▼
   Yuliana's Schedule C line 30  ──► line 31
        │
        ├──► Schedule 1 line 3  (both Schedule Cs combined)
        └──► Schedule SE  (one per spouse)

The three K-1s ──► Schedule E page 2 ──► Schedule 1 line 5
Yuliana's Form 7203 ──► (basis, and the basis for the sale)
The sale of Gossip ──► Form 8949 ──► Schedule D ──► 1040 line 7a
                                                       │
         Schedule 1 line 10 ─────────────────────────► 1040 line 8
                                                       ▼
                                          1040 line 9 → 11a  AGI
                                                       │
                            ┌──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┐
                            ▼                          ▼                      ▼
                     Form 8962 (PTC)          Schedule 8812 (CTC)        Form 8995 (QBI)
```

⚠️ **The circularity is Form 8829**: it wants Schedule C line 29, which is not final until Form 8829
comes back with line 36. **Do Schedule C Part II first, leave line 30 empty, then Form 8829, then
return.**

⚠️ **A second one, less obvious:** Form 8962 needs AGI, and AGI depends on every schedule above it —
so **the 1095-A is keyed LAST**, not first.

### 3E · FORM 8829 — YULIANA'S HOME OFFICE 🗺️ *Form 8829, **page 1**, Parts I and II*

> **What this form is FOR, in plain words.** A room used **only** for the business is partly a
> business cost. Form 8829 works out **what fraction of the home is the office** (Part I), then
> applies that fraction to **the whole home's running costs** (Part II) — and **caps the answer at
> the profit the business actually made**, so a home office can never create or deepen a loss.
> ⛔ **That cap is why the form wants Schedule C line 29 on its line 8, and it is the reason the two
> forms have to be done in the right order.**

**Part I — the fraction**

| Line | ⌨️/ƒ | Concept | Value | **Where it came from** | 🛠️ Entered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | ⌨️ | Area used regularly **and exclusively** for business | **474** sq ft | 📖 home-office sheet, `Sq of Office Space` | typed |
| **2** | ⌨️ | Total area of home | **1,580** sq ft | 📖 same sheet, `Total Sq of House/ Apt` | typed |
| **3** | ƒ | line 1 ÷ line 2 | **30.00 %** | 🧮 474 ÷ 1,580 = 0.30 exactly | computed |
| **4–6** | — | daycare only | *do not arise* | | |
| **7** | ƒ | **Business percentage** | **30.00 %** | ƒ = line 3 | computed |

> 🟠 **THE EXCLUSIVE-USE TEST IS THE RISK ON THIS FORM, NOT THE ARITHMETIC.** 474 sq ft is **nearly
> a third of a 1,580 sq ft apartment occupied by a family of four**, and §280A(c)(1) requires the
> space be used **exclusively** for business — not a room that is also used for anything else.
> 🔑 **The firm has asked this exact question of another client three days ago**, in Julia's own
> words: *"SQ ft seems to be very high. Do you store equipment somewhere?"* **§6 item 4.**

**Part II — the deduction.** ⚠️ Column **(a)** is *direct* (spent only on the office) and column
**(b)** is *indirect* (the whole home). **Everything here is indirect.**

| Line | ⌨️/ƒ | Concept | Col | Value | **Where it came from** | 🛠️ Entered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **8** | ƒ | Schedule C line 29 | | 🔵 **13,506** | ƒ ← §3B line 29. 🔑 **The cap** — ✅ nowhere near binding at 5,321 | ⛔ arrives from Schedule C |
| **9** | — | Casualty losses | | **0** | none | |
| **10** | — | Deductible mortgage interest | | **0** | 🔑 **They RENT** | |
| **11** | — | Real estate taxes | | **0** | they rent | |
| **12–14** | ƒ | | | **0** | ƒ all zero because 9–11 are | computed |
| **15** | ƒ | = line 8 − line 14 | | 🔵 **13,506** | | computed |
| **16, 17** | — | Excess mortgage interest / RE taxes | | **0** | they rent | |
| **18** | ⌨️ | **Insurance** | (b) | **95** | 📖 home-office sheet, `Insurance` — 23.75 × 4 months (Sep–Dec). 🔴 **This is the 95 that must come OUT of Schedule C line 15** | typed |
| **19** | ⌨️ | **Rent** | (b) | 🔴 **17,080** | 📖 home-office sheet, `Rent Expense` — 4,270 × 4 months (Sep–Dec). ⛔ **The FULL amount, not the 5,124 her P&L already netted to 30%** | typed |
| **20** | — | Repairs and maintenance | (b) | **0** | none on the sheet | |
| **21** | ⌨️ | **Utilities** | (b) | **560** | 📖 home-office sheet, `Utilities` — 140 × 4 months. ⛔ **Full amount** | typed |
| **22** | — | Other expenses | | **0** | the sheet's Internet / Cleaning / HOA / Property Taxes / Mortgage Interest rows are all empty | |
| **23** | ƒ | Add 16–22 | (b) | **17,735** | 🧮 95 + 17,080 + 560 | computed |
| **24** | ƒ | line 23(b) × line 7 | | **5,321** | 🧮 **17,735 × 30.00% = 5,320.50**, rounded | computed |

| **28–33** | ƒ | Excess casualty / **depreciation of the home** | | **0** | 🔑 **They rent, so there is no home to depreciate.** Part III is left entirely blank | computed |

### 3F · ✅ THE VEHICLES — ANSWERED 2026-09-10, AND IT IS **ONE CAR**

> ✅ **BOTH MILEAGE ORGANIZERS CAME BACK on 2026-09-10** — `155334` (Marat) and `155333`
> (Yuliana), **both marked complete by Marat Bogopolskyy**, who filled in both. ⛔ **This was the
> last blocker that needed a figure.**

#### 🟠 THE FINDING: ONE CAR IS THE WORKING ASSUMPTION — AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS SECTION OVERSTATED IT

§3F used to say: *"Either there are two vehicles and the organizer answer is wrong, or there is one
vehicle and its costs are being split across two Schedule Cs."* **The evidence points hard at the
second. It does not establish it, and this paper claimed it did.**

> ⚠️ **THE CORRECTION, because the argument mattered more than the conclusion.** The first version
> offered *"three independent pieces of evidence"* and called the third decisive:
> *"5,536 + 1,846 + 3,693 = 11,075, the total to the mile."*
> ⛔ **That equation carries no information.** **3,693 is not a reported number** — this paper
> *defines* it as 11,075 − 5,536 − 1,846, so the sum is true by construction and would be true for
> **any** pair of business-mile figures. The same goes for *"Yuliana's other 9,229 = Marat's business
> + personal"*, which is 9,229 = 11,075 − 1,846 restated. **Strip it back and all it shows is that
> both forms used the same odometer readings — which is the second piece of evidence, not a third.**
> 🔴 **And the paper records the fact that undercuts the rest without connecting it: MARAT FILLED IN
> BOTH ORGANIZERS.** One person completing two forms is the most economical explanation for the same
> odometer on both **and** the same document attached to both — one set of numbers in his head, one
> file to hand. **Two cars with *coincidentally* identical readings at both ends is impossible; two
> cars with *copied* readings is not.**

**What the evidence actually is, strongest first:**

| | |
|---|---|
| 🔑 **The two P&Ls report ONE SET OF BILLS at exactly 3 : 1** | Insurance **3,468 = 3 × 1,156** and lease **7,716 = 3 × 2,572**, both exact. ✅ **It comes from a different record than the mileage forms** ⚠️ **— but it is NOT independent evidence in the strong sense, and the 2026-09-11 review was right to press it.** 🔑 **One bookkeeper produced BOTH P&Ls, and §3F ② concludes Yuliana's figures are literally Marat's ÷ 3 — a derived number carries no information about how many cars exist.** ⛔ **What it does establish is narrower and still useful: the two P&Ls describe ONE car's bills. A second car the books never saw is exactly what it cannot rule out** — which is why line 46 has to be **asked**, not inferred |
| **Same odometer, both ends** | **9,850** at the start and **20,925** at the end, on **both** forms. ⚠️ **Weakened by the single filler** |
| **Same document** | Both organizers carry **`33806941-Contract.pdf`**, and the two copies are **byte-identical** *(same SHA-256, verified 2026-09-11 without reading it)*. ⚠️ **Weakened the same way — one filler, one file to hand.** ⛔ **And note what this is NOT: one person uploading one file twice is exactly what two cars would also look like. It is not evidence of one CAR.** |
| ⓘ *Internal consistency* | Each form independently partitions the same 11,075-mile total. **Consistent with one car; not evidence of it** |

🛠️ **THE ONE-LINE QUESTION THAT CLOSES IT, and Schedule C line 46 requires the answer anyway:**
***"How many vehicles does the household have, and is any of this mileage on a second one?"***
**It goes in the same message as items 28, 29 and 30.**

**So the arithmetic is:**

| | Miles | ÷ 11,075 | |
|---|---|---|---|
| Total driven 2025 | **11,075** | | 🧮 20,925 − 9,850 |
| **Marat — business** | **5,536** | 🔴 **49.99%** | |
| **Yuliana — business** | **1,846** | **16.67%** | |
| Genuinely personal | **3,693** | 33.35% | |
| *(combined business)* | *7,382* | *66.65%* | |

⚠️ **A 2-mile discrepancy, recorded not chased:** Marat's two figures sum to 11,073, not 11,075.
Immaterial, and it is evidence these were **computed by hand rather than read off a log** — which
is §274(d)'s question, below.
🛠️ **One thing to say about it, because it reaches the FACE of the return** *(tax review,
2026-09-11)*: **Part IV will print 44a + 44b + 44c = 11,073 on Marat's**, two miles short of the
odometer difference the same worksheet states as 11,075. ⚠️ **Nothing turns on two miles** — but 47a
and 47b are already being asked on that same page, so **know which figure the software prints before
someone else notices the gap.** ✅ *Yuliana's 1,846 + 9,229 = 11,075 exactly.*

#### 🟠 THE 50% THRESHOLD — MARAT MISSES IT BY **TWO MILES**, and on a LEASE that costs nothing

🔒 **Read off Pub 463 (2025) 2026-09-10:** *"If your business use is 50% or less, you must use the
straight line method to depreciate your car"*, and *"You must use the property more than 50% for
business to claim any section 179 deduction."* **The test is MORE than 50%.**

**Half of 11,075 is 5,537.5, so it takes 5,538 business miles to pass. Marat reported 5,536.**

🔑 **Both spouses therefore fail it** — Marat at 49.99%, Yuliana at 16.67%. ✅ **And on THIS car it costs
nothing: a LEASED vehicle has no depreciation and no §179 for anyone at any percentage**, so the two
miles decide nothing on this return. ⚠️ **Kept for two reasons** — it is one of the facts that made
actual expenses obviously right, and **the moment they buy a car instead of leasing one it becomes
live.** ⛔ **The part below is NOT moot and governs this return:** ⛔ **And the two do NOT combine:** Pub 463 — *"Don't treat any use of your car by another person as
use in your trade or business **unless that use meets one of the following conditions: it is directly
connected with your business · it is properly reported by you as income to the other person · it
results in a payment of fair market rent**."* ✅ **None of the three is met** — her driving is her own
trade or business, nothing is reported to her, no rent is paid — **so Yuliana's 1,846 miles are not
Marat's qualified business use**, and the 66.65% combined figure has
no standing on either return. **The organizers were filled in correctly on exactly this point.**

#### ✅ THE CONTRACT IS A **LEASE** — read off it by Lilian, 2026-09-11, and it settles four things at once

> ✅ **Mercedes-Benz Financial Services, *Consumer Leasing Act Disclosures*, page 2 of 11** — the same
> 11-page document this session hashed and could not read. **Lilian read it and supplied the figures.**

| From the disclosure | |
|---|---|
| Signed | **29 May 2024** · first payment 05/29/2024, then **35** more on the 29th — **36 payments** |
| **Monthly payment** | 🔵 **643.89** *(base 601.77 + 42.12 monthly sales tax)* |
| Agreed-upon value of the vehicle | **47,560.86** · gross cap cost 48,655.86 |
| Capitalized cost reduction | **2,500.00** *(+ 88.24 of tax — ⚠️ **the LESSOR's own grouping; ③ below proves the 88.24 is tax on the DEALER FEES, not on the reduction**)* → adjusted cap cost 46,155.86 |
| Residual | 30,002.85 · purchase option 30,002.85 + 150 fee |
| **Mileage allowance** | 🔴 **30,000 miles for the 36-month term**, excess at **$0.25/mile** |
| Due at signing | 4,722.63 |

**① ⌨️ THE 2025 LEASE DEDUCTION IS A REAL FIGURE NOW.** Payments fall on the 29th, so **twelve land in
2025**: 12 × 643.89 = **7,726.68**.

**② ✅ IT RESOLVES §6 ITEM 29 — AND THE ANSWER IS NOT THE ONE THIS PAPER GUESSED.** Marat's P&L
carries `Rent/Lease car` at **7,716**. ⚠️ **Twelve contractual payments are 12 × 643.89 = 7,726.68**,
so his figure is **10.68 short of the whole year — near enough that it IS the whole year**, rounded or
one cent-level adjustment out, and **nowhere near a 75% share** *(which would be 5,795)*. Yuliana's
**2,572 is 7,716 ÷ 3 exactly**, and her insurance **1,156 is 3,468 ÷ 3** the same way.
⛔ **The pattern is not a 75/25 split of one bill: Marat records the TOTAL and Yuliana records a THIRD
of that same total. Her vehicle lines are DOUBLE COUNTING.**
🔑 **On the ÷3 LINES the cost base is MARAT'S figure alone. Adding those would inflate them by a third.**
🔴 **⚠️ READ ⑥ BEFORE APPLYING THIS TO EVERY LINE — it does NOT reach the fuel.** 456 is **not**
1,583 ÷ 3, so it is not the same duplicate presentation, and **Lilian's own statement of the rule sums
the two.** ⛔ **This block was written before her second annotation and its "every vehicle line" reading
is superseded on that one line.**
*(It still kills the old six-branch table's **C1** — the actual-expense branch that added the two spouses' figures **on the ÷3 lines too**. That table is gone; what replaced it is the two-column B-vs-C comparison below.)*
⚠️ **One honest limit: the LEASE line is CORROBORATED by the contract *(10.68 apart, not equal)*; the INSURANCE line has no document at all.**
There is no insurance document in hand — **3,468 is taken as the year's premium because it follows the
identical ÷3 pattern and sits on the same two P&Ls**, which is an inference from the bookkeeping, not a
reading of a policy. 🛠️ **§6 item 29 is now ONLY that question** — ✅ its fuel half closed on 2026-09-11 *(⑥)*, and what
remains is *"what did the car insurance cost you for the whole year?"*.

**③ 🛑 THE ADVANCE PAYMENT IS *NOT* TAKEN — LILIAN'S DECISION, 2026-09-11, AND THE REASON IS THE RIGHT
ONE: THE RULE IS VERIFIED AND THE CLASSIFICATION IS NOT.**

> ✅ **Her words:** *"Vamos a utilizar solamente $7,726. No vamos a utilizar esa parte de los $2,500
> pagados… No vamos a complicar las cosas si no estamos seguros."*

🔒 **WHAT IS VERIFIED — Pub 463, *Leasing a Car* → *Deductible payments*, read at source 2026-09-11:**
*"You must spread any **advance payments** over the entire lease period."* ⛔ **It says *must*; that half
is not a judgement.**
🟠 **WHAT IS NOT VERIFIED — that the 2,500 capitalized cost reduction IS an "advance payment" for that
sentence.** ⛔ **The lease document does not say so.** It discloses two facts and no more: a
**capitalized cost reduction of 2,500**, and a **36-month term.** **It never calls it prepaid rent and
never says to spread it.**
🔑 **The bridge was this paper's, not a source's:** the cap cost reduction lowers the adjusted
capitalized cost and therefore lowers the base rent, so it is money paid up front that buys down future
rent — **which is what would make it rent paid in advance.** ⚠️ **That is a reading.**
🛑 **AND THE RIVAL READING DOES *NOT* SURVIVE — established 2026-09-11, AFTER Lilian ruled, by reading
the sources §6 item 31 had listed as unread.** ⛔ **This paper told her the question was open in both
directions. It is not, and she decided on that description.**
🔒 **Pub 334 (2025), chapter 8, *Rent Expense*, read at source:** *"**Rent paid in advance.** …If you pay
rent in advance, you can deduct only the amount that applies to your use of the rented property during
the tax year. **You can deduct the rest of your payment only over the period to which it applies.**"*
🔒 **Pub 334 (2025), chapter 8, *Prepayment* — and note it is not limited to rent:** *"You can't deduct
expenses in advance, even if you pay them in advance. This rule applies to **any expense** paid far
enough in advance to, in effect, create an asset with a useful life extending substantially beyond the
end of the current-tax year."* *(Its own example is a 3-year insurance contract, deducted a year at a
time.)*
🔒 **Pub 334 (2025), chapter 2, *Cash Method*:** *"**Expenses paid in advance.** You can deduct an expense
you pay in advance only in the year to which it applies."*
🔒 **Pub 538, *Expenses Paid in Advance*:** *"An expense you pay in advance is deductible only in the year
to which it applies, **unless the expense qualifies for the 12-month rule**"* — and the 12-month rule
reaches only benefits **not extending beyond 12 months after they begin.**
🔑 **A payment made at the start of a THIRTY-SIX-MONTH lease cannot meet the 12-month rule under any
characterisation.** ⛔ **So *"rent paid in advance"* and *"a cost of entering the lease"* land on the SAME
answer — spread over the term — and the reading that put all 2,500 into 2024 has no support.**
⚠️ **The only reading left that gives nothing in any year is that the 2,500 is not a deductible cost of
the lease at all**, which this paper never argued and no source supports.
🛑 **THIS IS BACK IN FRONT OF HER — §6 item 31 — because her ruling was conditional on the uncertainty
*("si no estamos seguros")*, and the uncertainty is smaller than she was told.** ⛔ **The return stays as
she ruled until she says otherwise; a session may not reason its way back into a position she closed.**

🛑 **THIS PAPER CARRIED IT AS ✅ FOR A DAY, AND THAT LABEL WAS WRONG.** *(Caught when Lilian asked the
plain question — "¿dónde lo leíste, en el documento de renta o dónde?" — and the honest answer was "the
rule in the Pub, the classification in my own head.")* ✅ **She then ruled it out rather than leaving an
inference inside a signed return, which is the correct call on a 79-dollar item.**

⚠️ **WHAT IT COSTS AND WHAT IT LEAVES OPEN.** The base falls from 8,560.01 to **7,726.68**, the refund
from **6,594** to **6,515**, and 🔴 **the whole 2,500 is now unclaimed in every year** — 2024's 555.56 as
well. 🛠️ **§6 item 31 carries what would settle it and what it is worth in 2026 and 2027**, because the
same question returns next year with two more years of the same 833.33 behind it.

> 🛑 **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 after the independent review, and the correction is provable off the
> contract — kept because it is why the 2,588.24 figure will not come back.** This section first used
> **2,588.24** — the 2,500 **plus the 88.24 of sales tax**, on the assumption the tax was levied on the
> cap cost reduction. **It is not.** The disclosure fixes the tax rate at **7%**: 601.77 × 7% = **42.12**,
> the disclosed monthly tax, to the cent. Apply it: **2,500 × 7% = 175.00**, not 88.24 — but
> **(predelivery 995 + tag agency 199 + FL fees 66.50) = 1,260.50 × 7% = 88.235 → 88.24**, exact.
> ⛔ **So the 88.24 is tax on the DEALER FEES.**

🟠 **THE SIGNING FEES ARE THREE BUCKETS, NOT ONE, AND ONLY THE MIDDLE ONE IS ARGUABLE** — §6 item 31:
| | | |
|---|---|---|
| **Prepaid rent** | cap cost reduction **2,500** | 🛑 **NOT TAKEN — Lilian, 2026-09-11.** ⛔ **833.33 would have belonged to 2025; it is left out** *(§4 decision 15)* |
| 🟠 **Lease acquisition cost** | predelivery **995** + tag agency **199** *(+ the 83.58 of tax on those two)* | **arguably amortisable** — **1,277.58 ÷ 36 × 12 = 425.86**, so ≈ **426** more on the line-20a base, ≈ **284** of actual deduction once each spouse's percentage is applied. ⛔ **Not taken** |
| ⛔ **Licence and registration** | title **230** *(untaxed)* + FL fees **66.50** *(+ 4.66 of tax)* | 🔒 **Pub 463 lists these as ordinary actual car expenses, deductible WHEN PAID** — so they belong to **2024**, not to 2025, and are **not amortisable**. ⚠️ **Whether they are "lost" depends on §6 item 28:** on a **Yes** *(the car was in business use in 2024)* they were deductible then and are lost absent an amendment; on a **No** they were **personal costs that were never deductible at all** — nothing was lost |
ⓘ **One piece of evidence for the wider reading, recorded because it is real:** the lessor's own
**Total of Payments** reconciles only on the whole signing block — **4,078.74 + 23,180.04 + 595.00 =
27,853.78**, the disclosed figure to the cent. ⚠️ **That is the LESSOR's grouping for disclosure, not
a tax characterisation** — but it is why this is a judgement rather than an oversight.

**④ ✅ NO INCLUSION AMOUNT — and the right measure is not the one this paper first used.**

income off the edge of the EIC plateau.** Standard mileage left 43 dollars of headroom against the
statutory threshold *(73 against the table's bracket)*, so §6 item 3
(Marat's 6,936 of `Education`) could push them over on its own. **At 26,551 there is real room** —
and it is still above **25,167**, where the ACTC would stop being capped and start falling, though
**only by 1,384 now** *(it was 868 on the superseded base, and 1,150 before that)*. ⚠️ **Column B does not move with the cost
base at all** — the standard mileage rate is 70¢ × business miles + parking, and neither changed — **so
the whole of that movement is on the actual-expense side.** ⓘ *The gap has run 624 → 668 → **589**, and ⛔ **the causes were stated wrongly here until the tax review on 2026-09-11** *(it said the gap "grew when the advance was amortised") — **the advance was amortised in the 624 state too** (§3F ⑥'s own table).* 🧮 **It grew 44 when the FUEL base was corrected** *(§4 decision 13)* **and fell 79 when Lilian removed the advance** *(§4 decision 15)*. ⚠️ **589 is SMALLER than 624, so the last step is a worsening, not an improvement** — actual expenses is still the better method by a wide margin.*

Boxing in 2024?* — resolves to actual expenses **either way**:
- **NO** → 2025 is the first business year, **both methods are available**, and actual expenses is

#### ⌨️ WHAT TO KEY — Schedule C lines 9 and 20a, both returns

**The base is THE CAR'S TOTAL COST** *(⑤ ⑥ above — Lilian's rule, in her own words, 2026-09-11)*:
lease **7,726.68** to line 20a — 🛑 **the twelve contractual payments and NOTHING ELSE; the advance is
deliberately NOT amortised, §4 decision 15** — and **gas and repairs 2,814**
*(fuel 1,583 + 456, the two cards SUMMED, + repairs 775)* + insurance **3,468** = **6,282** to line 9.
🔑 **Three of those four lines come from Marat's P&L alone because Yuliana's are duplicate thirds of
his; fuel is the one that is summed, because hers is not a third of anything** *(⑥)*.

🔴 **AND ONE LINE THE RULE DOES NOT REACH — PARKING AND TOLLS, WHICH STAY EACH PERSON'S OWN AT 100%.**
📖 **Marat 1,986, Yuliana 242, each from that spouse's own `Parking & Tolls` line.** ⛔ **They are not
pooled and not percentaged.** 🔑 **The reason is what the two kinds of cost ARE.** Fuel, insurance,
repairs and the lease are costs of the **car as a whole** — nobody can say which mile they paid for, so
the law splits them by the only evidence there is, the mileage ratio. **A parking receipt is the
opposite: it belongs to ONE trip, made by ONE of them, and you already know whose and why.** There is
nothing to allocate. 🔴 **THE AUTHORITY IS NOT THE ONE THIS PAPER FIRST CITED, and the tax review was right to say so.** It
leaned on *"parking fees you pay to park your car at your place of work are nondeductible commuting
expenses"* — ⛔ **that sentence settles WHICH parking is deductible, not what FRACTION of a year's
parking line is**, and it was carrying a 🔒 it had not earned.
✅ **The real support is that the IRS puts parking and tolls OUTSIDE the percentage wherever it gives an
instruction.** 🔒 **Pub 463 chapter 6, read at source 2026-09-11:** *"Show your transportation expenses
that didn't involve overnight travel on **Form 2106, line 2**, column A. **Also, include on this line
business expenses you have for parking fees and tolls. Don't include expenses of operating your car**…"*
— **line 2 is in Part I, outside Section C and outside the Part II business-use percentage.**
🔒 **And the 2025 Schedule C line 9 instruction does the same under the standard rate:** *"Multiply the
number of business miles driven by 0.70… **Add to this amount your parking fees and tolls**"* — no
percentage anywhere.
🔴 **AND THE TEXT AGAINST THE POSITION, quoted in full because it is the strongest thing on the other
side and belongs in front of whoever decides.** 🔒 **Pub 463's *Actual Car Expenses* list names "Tolls,
Parking fees" among actual car expenses, and the sentence immediately under it reads:** *"**Business and
personal use.** If you use your car for both business and personal purposes, you **must** divide your
expenses between business and personal use. You can divide your expense based on the miles driven for
each purpose."* ⛔ **A mandatory "must divide", directly under a list that names parking, is not a
technicality — it is the operative rule for the method this return is on.**
🔑 **What decides it, on this paper's reading:** the "must divide" sentence exists because nobody can say
which mile a tank of fuel bought. **A parking receipt is traceable to one trip**, and the two provisions
above show the IRS treating it that way in both places it tells anyone where to put one.
⚠️ **Say what those two places ARE** *(tax review, 2026-09-11)*: **Form 2106 line 2** and **Schedule C
line 9's *"add to this amount your parking fees and tolls"***. 🔑 **The actual-expense instruction to
Schedule C line 9 says nothing about parking at all**, so the position rests on those two plus the
traceability argument, **not on an instruction addressed to this line.**
✅ **BUT DO NOT DISMISS FORM 2106 LINE 2 AS "the employee route" — this paper did for part of a day and
it gave away its own best evidence.** 🔑 **Pub 463 ch. 6's *Transportation expenses* paragraph is NOT
limited to the standard rate:** an employee claiming **ACTUAL** expenses completes Part II Section C
for the car **and still puts parking on Part I line 2, outside the percentage.** ⛔ **That is the one
place the IRS routes parking for an actual-expense claimant** — a different taxpayer, the same method,
and it puts parking outside the business-use ratio.
ⓘ **ATX works the same way:** the Vehicle Expenses worksheet has its own parking field that it adds
**after** applying line 7, which is why Yuliana's 242 showed at 100% on her sheet.
⚠️ **AND THE POSITION CARRIES THE HEAVIER SUBSTANTIATION BURDEN, which whoever signs should hear:**
claiming **100%** of Marat's 1,986 on a car that is 49.99% business, under a per-receipt test, with
**§274(d) open** *(§6 item 32)* and no list of business trips, needs **each receipt to be a business
trip**. ⛔ **§6 item 30 frames this only as "is any of it commuting" — that is narrower than the real
burden.**
⚠️ **What pooling them would cost:** 2,228 pooled and percentaged gives Marat **1,114** against his own
1,986 and Yuliana **371** against her own 242 — **743 of deduction gone, about 104 of refund**, and
neither figure would match either person's records. 🛑 **§4 decision 14; the alternative is priced in
§5.** ⚠️ **§6 item 30 still asks whether any of Marat's 1,986 is commuting parking** — **that** is the
real exposure on this line, and it is a question about the trips, not about the split.

| \+ parking and tolls | ⌨️ | **1,986** | **242** |

> ### 🔴 YULIANA'S BUSINESS BEGAN IN SEPTEMBER — SO WHY IS HER BASE THE WHOLE YEAR?
>
> 🛑 **Three records say her business is a part-year one**: Schedule C box **H** is ticked *(began in
> 2025)*, Part IV line **43** says **09/01/2025**, and her home-office sheet covers **four months**
> *(rent 4,270 × 4)*. ⚠️ **And the table above applies her 16.67% to TWELVE months of car cost.**
> **The 2026-09-11 review raised this, and it is the right question to ask.**
>
> ✅ **The keyed figure survives it, and the reason is that the proration is ALREADY INSIDE the
> percentage.** Her 16.67% is **1,846 business miles ÷ 11,075 miles for the whole year** — the
> denominator is the full year, so the ratio is *already* the share of the full year's driving that
> was her business. Multiplying the full year's cost by it is therefore the same computation as
> taking her window's cost times her share *of that window*:
>
> | Route | Arithmetic | Line 20a |
> |---|---|---|
> | **Full year × full-year ratio** *(as keyed)* | 7,726.68 × 16.67% | **1,288** |
> | **Her window × her ratio in that window** | 4 payments **2,575.56**, × **50%** *(1,846 of an assumed ≈3,692 Sep–Dec miles)* | **1,288** |
> | ⛔ **The WRONG combination** | her window's cost **2,575.56** × the **full-year** 16.67% | **429** — *the proration applied twice* |
>
> 🔑 **The two routes agree to the dollar — but only ONE of them is right, and the tax review was
> correct that calling both "right" overstates it.** ✅ **The correct computation is the second: her
> window's cost × her share of driving IN that window.** 🟠 **The first — full year × full-year ratio —
> is an APPROXIMATION that coincides with it**, and it coincides only because the miles and the costs
> are assumed to run **evenly** across the year. ⚠️ **That assumption is the real exposure, not the base:**
> if she drove the car harder in the four months her business existed than in the other eight, her
> true share of that window is below 50% and 1,288 is too high; if lighter, too low.
> 🛠️ **What would settle it is one more number — the odometer at 1 September** — and nobody has it.
> **It rides with §6 item 29's message.** ⓘ *Marat's side does not raise this: his business ran all
> year.*
> ⓘ **2026-09-11 — the WORKSHEET and the PRINTED RETURN say different things, and only one of them is
> a line the taxpayer signs.** Yuliana's Vehicle Expense worksheet carries **`Date vehicle placed in
> service: 1/1/2025`** and **total miles 11,075**, which is the full-year presentation the base runs on.
> ✅ **But her printed Schedule C Part IV line 43 reads 09/01/2025** *(§3L ⑤)* — **her own organizer
> answer, and §4 decision 10.** 🔑 **They are not the same field**: the worksheet's date drives a
> computation, line 43 states a fact about **her business**, which began in September.
> 🛑 **This paper used to key line 43 at 01/01/2025 "because the base does". That was tailoring a signed
> fact to a computation and it is reversed** — ⛔ **and the base never needed it**, as the two routes
> above agree to the dollar either way.
> 🔑 **The CAR was available all year; what began in September was her BUSINESS** — which is why the
> full-year presentation is defensible and the odometer question is about precision, not principle.

🛑 **THE FUEL LINE MOVED TWICE ON 2026-09-11, AND BOTH MOVES WERE LILIAN'S — the history is kept
because the second one reverses the first and a session reading only the figure would not know that.**

| | The base | Refund | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Morning** | fuel **2,039** *(1,583 + 456, summed)* + repairs 775 = **2,814** | 6,594 | this paper's own reading: 456 is not 1,583 ÷ 3, so the two are separate receipts |
| **Midday** | fuel **1,583** *(Marat's alone)* + repairs 775 = **2,358** | 6,550 | ⛔ **her first annotation, *"tomé el valor mayor"***, applied to every line — **merged, and wrong on this one line** |
| **Afternoon** | fuel **2,039** + repairs 775 = **2,814** | 6,594 | ✅ **her SECOND annotation and the rule behind it** — *"los gastos totales deben ser iguales… según el porcentaje de uso… será la deducción"* |
| ✅ **Now** | **same fuel base** · ⛔ **the advance removed from line 20a** | ✅ **6,515** | ✅ **§4 decision 15, later the same day** — a different question from the fuel, and the only reason this row differs from the one above |

⚠️ **THE FOURTH ROW IS NOT ABOUT THE FUEL.** ⛔ **All three refunds above carry the amortised advance**;
the drop to 6,515 is decision 15 alone. *(The first three read 6,515 / 6,550 / 6,515 until the coherence
review caught it — a sweep had moved the refunds without moving the bases beside them, which made a
LARGER deduction show a SMALLER refund.)*

🔑 **The round trip is not indecision on her part; it is the difference between a rule of thumb and the
rule.** *"Take the larger figure"* gives the right answer wherever one spouse's line is a **duplicate**
of the other's — which is true of insurance and the lease, where hers is **exactly a third** of his.
⛔ **It gives the wrong answer where the two lines are DIFFERENT PAYMENTS for the same car**, which is
what fuel is: 456 is not a third of 1,583, so nothing was double-counted and the car burned both.
✅ **Her second message states the general rule and the general rule sums them.**
⛔ **What never survived either version is 456 alone**, which is what ATX still holds *(⑤)*.
✅ **The FUEL question is closed by her, not deferred — and §6 item 29 now carries only the INSURANCE.**

> ### 🛑 THE FIX HAS TWO FAILURE MODES, AND BOTH ARE SILENT — do this in ORDER
> *(Both found by the independent tax review, 2026-09-11. Neither is obvious from the screen.)*
>
> **① ⛔ CLEAR WORKSHEET LINE 15 FIRST. Typing 3,863 on line 20a without clearing it DEDUCTS THE LEASE
> TWICE** — once inside the computed line 9, once on line 20a — **about 5,700 of phantom deduction
> across the two returns.** 🔑 **Nothing on the screen shows it:** line 9 is a computed output, so it
> simply gets bigger, and no diagnostic fires. **Clear 15, then type 20a, then read both back.**
>
> **② 🔴 THE WORKSHEET'S OWN STANDARD-vs-ACTUAL VERDICT FLIPS THE MOMENT YOU OBEY THE INSTRUCTION.**

> so it compares a lease-less Actual against the full mileage rate:

> | **Yuliana** | **1,534** | 1,289 | 🔵 **2,577** |

> advance against what their **actual** income entitled them to, and settles the difference either
> way. 🔑 **It is the largest single figure on this return by an order of magnitude** — and it
> settles to **zero in both directions.**

**What the 1095-A says.** Policy issuer **Oscar Insurance Company of Florida**, Florida Marketplace,
**1 January – 31 December 2025**, and **all four family members covered for all twelve months**.

form and are deliberately not written here or anywhere else.* **Every month is identical:**

> ### ✅ THE ANSWER: NOTHING GOES ANYWHERE. NO REPAYMENT, NO CREDIT.
>
> **Schedule 3 line 9 = nothing. Schedule 2 line 1a = nothing.** 🔑 **The advance was exactly right** —
> the government paid 19,494.12 during the year, and their actual income entitled them to 19,494.12.
> ⛔ **This is not luck: at 80% of the poverty line the applicable figure is ZERO, so the credit is
> capped only by the premium itself, and the advance already equalled it.**
>
> 🔵 **AND THE RESULT IS ROBUST TO EVERY OPEN ITEM ON THIS RETURN.** The applicable figure stays
> **0.0000 all the way to 150% of the poverty line, which is household income of 46,800.** Their AGI
> is **25,083 — 80%**, and even the largest single swing on the return — Development Strategies turning
> out passive (§4 decision 5) — takes it to about **43,305, which is 138%** and ✅ **does not cross the
> 150% line.**
> ⚠️ **THIS ROW HAS MOVED THREE TIMES, and the history is the useful part, not the number.** On the
> client's own vehicle figures the passive branch reached ≈144% and this was called robust. **Standard
> mileage lifted AGI and the branch reached ≈151% — crossing 150% for the first time**, which was
> computed rather than assumed: applicable figure **0.0004**, contribution 18.86, and an excess-APTC
> repayment of **$11**. ✅ **The lease figures take AGI back down and the crossing is gone.**
> 🔑 **Carry the mechanism, not the verdict: the 150% line is close enough to this household that any
> change upstream can put them over it, so RE-COMPUTE rather than reuse.**
>
> 🔴 **AND THE VEHICLE ANSWER PUSHED THEM THE OTHER WAY HERE, which matters for §6 item 25.** Household
> income has run **86%** *(the client's own figures)*, then **92%** *(standard mileage)*, and now
> **80%** on actual expenses. ⛔ **Every extra dollar of car deduction moves them FURTHER BELOW 100%
> of the poverty line**, and it is the **below-100% exception** that makes them applicable taxpayers at
> all. ⚠️ **So the method that is worth 589 of refund also makes §6 item 25 matter MORE, not less** —
> 100% of FPL is household income of 31,200 and they are **6,117 under it**.
> 🛑 **AND DO NOT WAVE IT AWAY THE WAY THIS PAPER FIRST DID.** It said the exposure was capped at 750
> *"and the method is worth more than that"*. ⛔ **False on its own figures: the method is worth 589
> and the cap is 750**, so the two genuinely have to be weighed rather than dismissed. 🔑 **That is
> exactly why item 25 cannot be left to the end** *(tax review, 2026-09-11)*.
>
> ⚠️ **The main thing that could still change this is NOT a figure — it is §6 item 25** *(and item 25 now carries TWO other ways it closes)*, whether the
> under-100%-of-FPL exception is met. **If it is not, they are not applicable taxpayers, the PTC is
> 0, and the whole 19,494.12 becomes excess.** 🔒 **Table 5 then caps the repayment at $750 — and the
> 2026-09-11 review was right to make this earn its place, so it was read at source again.** The Line 28
> instruction is unconditional except for **two** named carve-outs, and *"not an applicable taxpayer"*
> is **not** one of them: it drops the limitation only when **line 5 is 400 or more** *(theirs is 80)*
> and for APTC paid for an **individual who is not lawfully present**. ✅ **So on the form's own
> mechanics the cap holds.**
> 🔴 **BUT THAT SECOND CARVE-OUT LANDS ON THIS FAMILY, AND IT IS THE SAME FACT ITEM 25 ALREADY TURNS ON.**
> *"They cannot take the PTC for their own coverage and are not eligible for the repayment limitations
> in Table 5 for APTC paid for their own coverage."* ⛔ **So the 750 is the ceiling only if everyone on
> the policy is lawfully present.** ⚠️ **Item 25's route ② is the "Alien lawfully present" exception —
> which means status is already the live question, and the ANSWER cuts both ways: lawfully present
> opens the PTC AND keeps the cap; not lawfully present closes the PTC AND removes the cap for that
> person's share.**
> 🔑 **So: the exposure is 0 to 750 on the facts this paper holds, and it is NOT bounded at 750 until
> lawful presence is confirmed for all four.** *(Pub 974 carries the apportionment where only some of
> the family are lawfully present; nobody has needed it yet.)*
>
> ✅ **AND IT CLOSES §6 ITEM 23 — the self-employed health insurance deduction is ZERO** *(⚠️ closed
> **conditionally**: see the last paragraph)*. 🔒 **Read off Pub 974 (2025) on 2026-09-10, because the
> obvious shorthand is NOT the rule.** **Two separate caps** apply and here **both are zero**:
> ① **Worksheet W line 3 = specified premiums less the ADVANCE** → 19,494.12 − 19,494.12 = **0**
> *(this is the "nothing out of pocket" limit)*; ② **Step 3 Worksheet line 7 = specified premiums less
> the PTC ALLOWED** → 19,494.12 − 19,494.12 = **0**, and the publication's governing sentence is that
> *"the sum of the deduction claimed for the premiums and the PTC computed… is less than or equal to
> the enrollment premiums."* 🔑 **The two caps are NOT the same rule and come apart the moment the
> credit ALLOWED differs from the ADVANCE** — they coincide here only because line 24 equals line 25.
> ⛔ **So do not write down "nothing was paid, so nothing is deductible" as the reason**; the reason
> is that the credit allowed absorbs the whole enrollment premium. **Schedule 1 line 17 stays empty**,
> and Pub 974's iteration has nothing to iterate on.
>
> ⚠️ **AND ITEM 23 IS CONTINGENT ON ITEM 25, WHICH IS STILL OPEN.** In the branch item 25
> contemplates — the exception fails, they are not applicable taxpayers, **PTC = 0** — cap ② stops
> binding altogether and the repaid **750** is itself a premium paid, so the deduction is **not
> zero**; Pub 974's method is also unavailable in that branch *(its Step 2 says to stop and use
> Form 7206 instead)*. 🔑 **It is worth almost no tax here** — taxable income is already nil and the
> EIC sits on its plateau — **but "zero" is only true on the branch this paper assumes.**

> 🛠️ **ENTRY ROUTE.** ⚠️ **Not established for ATX by this session.** In a forms-based program the
> 1095-A is keyed on **its own input sheet** and **Form 8962 is a computed output**. ✅ **Add the
> 1095-A form by name and type into that sheet, not into Form 8962** — if a line on 8962 refuses
> input, that is the confirmation, not a fault. 🔑 **The sheet will want the policy number, the
> issuer and the covered individuals** — all four are on the form in front of you; ⛔ **they are not
> written in this paper.**
> ✅ **Read back:** Form 8962 line 26 should print **-0-**, lines 27–29 empty of any real figure, and
> **nothing should appear on Schedule 2 line 1a or Schedule 3 line 9.** ⓘ *The fourth draft prints

### 3H · SCHEDULE E PAGE 2 — THE THREE K-1s 🗺️ *Schedule E (Form 1040), **page 2**, Part II*

> 🗺️ **READ THIS BEFORE HUNTING FOR A FIELD. Part II is TWO GRIDS on one page.**
> **Line 28** is the **identity grid** — name, P or S, foreign, EIN, and two checkboxes — and it has
> **no money column at all**. The money sits in a **second grid directly below it**, with **no line
> number of its own** and **the same row letters A/B/C**. ⛔ *"Schedule E page 2 Part II"* names a
> page with two places to write.

> 🆕 **AS-KEYED, 2026-09-11 — this section is RIGHT and the fourth draft does not follow it.** ATX
> put **`Development Strategies` in row B and `PEP Hollywood` in row C** *(the order is arbitrary;
> nothing turns on it)*. ✅ **Development Strategies is now `18,220`, fixed in the fifth draft**
> *(the fourth had 18,200)*. 🔴 **PEP's `20,699` is still in column (i)** — it should be nothing, and
> if anything it is column (g). **§3Q ④A carries the route.**
> ✅ **Column (e) IS ticked on the Gossip row.** 🛠️ **The two fixes are §3P ②B.**

| | **Line 28 — the identity grid** | | | | | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Row | **(a) Name** | **(b) P or S** | **(c) foreign** | **(d) EIN** | **(e) basis computation required** | **(f) any amount not at risk** |
| **A** | `GOSSIP MIAMI LLC` | **S** | ☐ | `99-2586917` | 🔴 **☑ TICK** | ☐ |
| **B** | `PEP HOLLYWOOD FL LLC` | **P** | ☐ | `99-0480864` | ☐ *(S corps only)* | 🟠 **CONDITIONAL — see below.** ⛔ **Do NOT tick on this paper's old reasoning** |
| **C** | `DEVELOPMENT STRATEGIES LLC` | **P** | ☐ | `33-2567480` | ☐ | ☐ |

**Why each box:** ✅ **(e) on row A** — the form's own note: *"If you report a loss, receive a
distribution, dispose of stock, or receive a loan repayment from an S corporation, you must check
the box in column (e) … and attach the required basis computation."* **She does two of those: a
distribution and a disposal.** 🔴 **(f) on row B — THE REASON THIS PAPER GAVE IS BACKWARDS ON THE LAW, and the tax review caught it
on 2026-09-11.** It read: *"his basis in PEP is zero plus a 392 share of RECOURSE debt, so the loss is
not at risk."* ⛔ **Recourse debt is the textbook AT-RISK amount, not the opposite.**
🔒 **§465(b)(2)(A) treats a taxpayer as at risk for borrowed amounts *"to the extent that he is
personally liable for the repayment"*, and the 2025 Schedule E instructions' own list of what you are
NOT at risk for — read at source 2026-09-11 — is exactly three things:** nonrecourse loans; amounts
*"protected against loss by a guarantee, stop-loss agreement, or other similar arrangement"*; and
amounts borrowed from a person with an interest in the activity. ⛔ **A recourse share under §752 is on
none of them** — it exists *because* the partner bears the economic risk of loss.
🛠️ **SO THE TICK IS NOT ESTABLISHED AND NEITHER IS FORM 6198. Ask the partnership how the 392 arose:**
a **direct recourse allocation** → ⛔ **leave (f) blank and do NOT file Form 6198**, keeping only Form
8582; a share arising from a **guarantee** → ✅ **(f) is right**, because Reg. §1.465-6(d) leaves a
guarantor not at risk until he actually pays. **It rides with §6 item 12.**
✅ **No money moves either way** — the 392 dies at §469 regardless *(§3H-1 ①)* — ⛔ **but column (f) is a
representation the TAXPAYER SIGNS, and ticking it is what MAKES Form 6198 a required attachment** —
⚠️ **§10 carries it as CONDITIONAL on exactly that tick, not as required outright; this line said §10
made it required until 2026-09-11.**

Worksheet is entirely empty and no Form 8582 existed in 2024.**

> 🛠️ **THE ENTRY ROUTE, AND IT IS THE THING THAT LOSES PEOPLE.**
> 🔴 **None of the money above is typed on Schedule E.** Each K-1 is keyed on **its own input form**,
> and **Schedule E page 2 is a computed OUTPUT** of those screens *(established at this firm on a
> live return in ATX, August 2026 — [`kolo-florida-inc/2025-form-1120s.md`](../kolo-florida-inc/2025-form-1120s.md)
> §8A and the [`tax-return-sop`](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/) §4B)*.
>
> | Which K-1 | **The form to add, by its exact name in the Add Forms box** |
> |---|---|
> | Gossip Miami *(S corporation)* | 🔴 **`K1 INPUT (1120S)`** |
> | PEP Hollywood · Development Strategies *(partnerships)* | 🔴 **`K1 INPUT (1065)`** |
>
> ⛔ **SAY WHAT IT IS NOT, because typing `K1` in a 1040 returns FOUR forms:** `K1 INPUT (1041)` ·
> `K1 INPUT (1065)` · `K1 INPUT (1120S)` · `SCH K1 (8865)`. **`SCH K1 (8865)` is the K-1 you ISSUE
> to a FOREIGN partnership's partners** — the wrong entity type and the wrong direction, and it is
> the one that has been picked by mistake before. 🔑 **The tell: `K1 INPUT …` asks for the
> COMPANY's name, EIN and address — one entity per sheet. `SCH K1 …` is a multi-owner GRID asking
> for the OWNERS' names.** *(Form names read off the vendor's own forms list, 2026-09-07; ⚠️ **the
> layout inside `K1 INPUT (1065)` has not been seen by this session** — screenshot it and it gets
> written in here.)*
>
> ⚠️ **And what still IS typed on Schedule E page 2:** **line 27's Yes/No**, and the **(e) and (f)
> ticks on line 28**. ⛔ **Do not mark the whole destination as computed** — those three are the
> preparer's.
>
> ### 🛠️ HOW A K-1 IS ACTUALLY KEYED IN ATX — researched 2026-09-11 at Lilian's request
>
> 🔑 **She asked for this in those words** — *"trata de encontrar instrucciones de cómo completarlos
> dentro de ATX, lo cual es diferente a completarlo en la forma del IRS; ATX tiene otra tabla y otra
> forma diferente."* ✅ **She is right that it is different, and the difference is the point:** the IRS
> Schedule K-1 is a **report**, ATX's `K1 INPUT` is a **questionnaire**, and the boxes do not line up
> one to one. 🔴 **Three things below are ESTABLISHED and one is REPORTED BUT UNVERIFIED — each is
> labelled, because keying from a guess is how a wrong figure gets signed.**
>
> **① 🟠 DO NOT TYPE THE GOSSIP MIAMI K-1 AT ALL — IMPORT IT.** 🔑 **The firm is preparing that 1120-S
> itself**, and ATX moves a K-1 straight from the business return into the 1040:
> `Returns` menu → **`Import Data`** → **`K-1 Data`** → a dialog lists every business return in which
> this person is a partner, shareholder or beneficiary and **pre-ticks them all** → untick the ones you
> do not want → **`Import Data For Marked Returns`** → **`Done`**. ✅ **The figures land on the
> applicable `K1 INPUT` worksheet in the 1040.** ⚠️ **It also works for an UPDATE** — change the K-1 in
> the 1120-S, reopen the 1040, run the same import again — which is exactly what §6 item 19 needs after
> the Gossip 1120-S is actually filed. 🟠 **WHERE THIS WAS ACTUALLY READ — because ④ below says the vendor's host is blocked, and the two
> statements have to be consistent.** The ATX help page *"Importing K-1 Data"* and CCH knowledge-base
> solution **2595** both sit on hosts this environment **cannot reach**. ⛔ **Neither page was opened.**
> **What was read is a search engine's rendering of their text**, which returned the menu path and the
> dialog's behaviour in the vendor's own words, and **two independent results agreed on the path** —
> which is why this is 🟠 **well-sourced rather than ✅ verified.** 🛠️ **One run of the import settles it
> in thirty seconds; if the menu is not there, say so and this gets rewritten.** ⛔ **PEP Hollywood and Development Strategies cannot be imported — the
> firm did not prepare those returns — so those two are typed.**
>
> **② ✅ ONE `K1 INPUT` SHEET PER ENTITY, AND THE FORM NAME DEPENDS ON THE ENTITY, NOT ON YOU.**
> `K1 INPUT (1120S)` for Gossip Miami; `K1 INPUT (1065)` for the two partnerships.
> ✅ **THAT — the names, and one sheet per entity — is what is established**, read off the vendor's forms
> list on 2026-09-07; §3H above says which four forms answer to `K1` and which one is the trap.
> 🛑 **WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED IS ANYTHING ABOUT THE INSIDE OF THE SHEET, and this block described it
> anyway until the tax review, 2026-09-11.** It said *"the box numbers follow the K-1 you are holding,
> so box 1 goes in box 1"* — ⛔ **a reasonable expectation, not a reading** — and it sat four paragraphs
> above this block's own admission that **nobody has ever seen inside `K1 INPUT (1065)`.**
> 🔑 **What IS safe to say without seeing it, because it is true of the DATA and not of the software:**
> the fields that cause trouble are the ones with **no number on the IRS form** — the entity's name and
> EIN, the partner-type tick, the activity's passive/nonpassive status, and the basis block. **Those are
> ③ and ④ below.**
>
> **③ 🔴 THE ONE FIELD THAT DECIDES WHERE THE MONEY LANDS IS NOT A BOX NUMBER — it is the activity's
> PASSIVE / NONPASSIVE designation on the input sheet.** ✅ **This is the mechanism behind §3H's whole
> money grid**, and it is common to every package:
> | What you tell the input sheet | Where the figure comes out |
> |---|---|
> | **Nonpassive / materially participated** | Schedule E line 28 column **(k)** if income, column **(i)** if a loss — **straight through, no limit** |
> | **Passive** | 🔴 **Form 8582 FIRST** — **Part V** *(⛔ **not "Worksheet 3"** — the 2025 form has **no worksheets at all**; see §3Q ④A)* — and only what 8582 allows reaches column **(g)** |
> 🔑 **So "the loss did not come through" is almost never a broken import: it is the activity marked
> passive with no passive income to absorb it.** ⚠️ **And that is exactly the PEP Hollywood answer** —
> ✅ **marking PEP passive is what produces the correct ZERO**, and it is how §4 decision 7 gets keyed
> without deleting anything. ⛔ **The §704(d) basis limit is a SEPARATE control from the passive one**;
> **both** apply to PEP, and the basis limit is the one that suspends 20,307.
>
> **④ ✅ FORM 7203 — SUPERSEDED 2026-09-11. READ §3I ⑤b INSTEAD.** 🟢 **The screenshot this section asks
> for ARRIVED**, and the route is now confirmed in ATX's own banner *(§3I ⑤b, §9)*. ⛔ **Do not cite this
> subsection as "never established" — §3I ⑥ and §3M ① did, and both are corrected.** ⚠️ **What remains
> unverified is narrower: the entry route for LINE 13 specifically.** *The original text is kept below
> as the history of how it was closed.*
>
> **④ 🟠 FORM 7203 — REPORTED, NOT VERIFIED AT SOURCE** *(as it stood before the screenshot)*. The route described in the field is: open
> **`K1 INPUT (1120S)`**, scroll **below the regular box-by-box inputs to the `Basis Limitation`
> section**, enter the opening stock basis and any contributions in the **`Regular`** column, and set
> the **`Link to form`** dropdown to **`Form 7203`**. ⛔ **THIS SESSION COULD NOT OPEN THE VENDOR PAGE
> THAT SAYS IT** — CCH's own help host is blocked by this environment's network policy, and the CCH
> knowledge-base article *(solution 000213713, "How do I enter an amount on Form 7203 in a 1040 return")*
> renders only through its browser app. 🔑 **So treat ④ as a place to look, not as an instruction**, and
> ⚠️ **the figures to key are §3I's regardless of where the fields turn out to be.**
> 🛠️ **One screenshot of that section closes it permanently** — send it and the labels get written in
> here, the way §3F's Vehicle Expenses worksheet was.
>
> ⚠️ **STILL NOT SEEN BY ANY SESSION: the inside of `K1 INPUT (1065)`.** ⛔ **This paper will not invent
> its field names.**
>
> ✅ **"It did not flow" — what to check on the printed form.** 🔴 **The first question is not
> *did it arrive* but *WHICH COLUMN did it land in*.** Line 30 adds **(h) passive income** and **(k)
> nonpassive income**, so **a K-1 classified the wrong way lands one column over and line 32 is
> identical** — the total will not tell you. **Read column by column, not just the total.**
> ⚠️ **On a LOSS the symmetry breaks** — a misclassified loss goes to **(g)** instead of **(i)**, and
> (g) only carries what Form 8582 allows, so there line 32 usually *does* move.

### 3H-1 · 📄 THE THREE K-1s, BOX BY BOX — including the boxes that are EMPTY

> 🔑 **A blank box is a fact about the entity, not an absence of information.** And on a K-1 the
> parts that decide the return are usually the ones with **no number in them** — the partner-type
> tick, the percentages, the capital account.

> The package includes a **`Partner's Basis Worksheet`** which reads, in its own boxes:
> beginning of year **0** · capital contributions **none** · change in liabilities **+392** ·
> **subtotal 392** · **`Deductible losses: Ordinary business loss` … 392** · **end of year 0.**
>
> | | Limit | Effect |
> |---|---|---|
> | **1** | **§704(d) — basis** | 🔒 **only 392 of the 20,699 is deductible. 20,307 is suspended** — and this is the partnership's own computation, not ours |
> | **2** | **§465 — at risk** | his 392 comes entirely from a **recourse liability**, and **§465(b)(2)(A) makes a recourse amount an AT-RISK amount** — ⛔ **but do NOT turn that into a default for THIS entity.** 🔴 **PEP HOLLYWOOD FL is an LLC, and an LLC member is not personally liable for the LLC's debts** *(tax review, 2026-09-11)*, **so a §752 RECOURSE share on an LLC member's K-1 usually arises BECAUSE of a guarantee or a deficit-restoration obligation** — which is exactly the branch that **does** require the tick and Form 6198 *(Reg. §1.465-6(d))*. 🔑 **So on this entity the recourse label is a HINT toward a guarantee, not away from one, and the question is UNDECIDABLE until §6 item 12 answers it.** ⛔ **Do not tick and do not leave blank by default.** ⚠️ *This row said the limit "does not bite and Form 6198 is not required" for part of 2026-09-11 — that was a default it could not support, and before that it said the opposite for the wrong reason; §3L ④ has had it right throughout by refusing to default either way* |
> | **3** | **§469 — passive** | a **limited partner** is presumed not to materially participate. The 392 goes to **Form 8582**, and 🔴 **there is no passive income to absorb it** — the Gossip income is **nonpassive** and cannot |
>
> ✅ **Net effect on the 2025 return: ZERO deduction. All 20,699 carries forward** — 20,307 as a
> basis carryforward and 392 as a suspended passive loss. ⚠️ **AND THE TWO ARE NOT EQUALLY SAFE:** the
> **392** is released against future passive income **or on a fully taxable disposal** *(§469(g))*,
> but the **20,307 is personal to the partner and is LOST if he disposes of the interest before basis
> is restored** — §3Q ③ *(Sennett, `unverified`)*.
> ⚠️ **The worksheet's own footnote is the caveat: *"This worksheet was prepared based on partnership
> records. Please consult with your tax advisor for adjustments."*** **If Marat put money in that the
> partnership did not record, his basis is higher and more of the loss is deductible. §6 item 12.**

### 3I · 🔴 YULIANA'S FORM 7203, AND THE SALE OF GOSSIP MIAMI

> **What Form 7203 is FOR, in plain words.** An S corporation's owner is taxed on the company's
> profit whether or not any money reaches them. This form keeps a running tally of **what that
> ownership is worth to them for tax** — what they put in, plus profits already taxed to them,
> minus what they took out. 🔑 **Two things depend on it: whether a distribution is taxable, and
> what the gain or loss is when the interest is SOLD.** This year it is the second one that matters.

> 🛑 **AS-KEYED BANNER, 2026-09-11 — EXTENDED THE SAME DAY, AFTER THE INDEPENDENT REVIEW FOUND IT
> UNDER-COVERED.** **Lilian keyed line 2 as `1,011`**, not the `1,010` this section computes — the ±1 of
> the pooled split, and hers is as defensible as ours *(this section's own line-2 note had already named
> 1,011 as the rounding)*.
> 🎯 **EVERY FIGURE BELOW THAT IS ONE DOLLAR LOW, in full — the first version listed only the first four
> and left the SALE table underneath it reading `85,801` and `(28,801)` AS INSTRUCTION, under a heading
> telling the preparer to read the basis back off it:**
>
> | Reads below | **Reads on the return** |
> |---|---|
> | line 5 `107,134` | **`107,135`** |
> | line 7 `86,434` | **`86,435`** |
> | line 10 `85,801` | **`85,802`** |
> | line 13 `85,801` | **`85,802`** — ⌨️ **the one that must be TYPED.** ✅ **DONE in the fifth draft** *(the fourth held 85,801)* |
> | 8949 **cost basis** `85,801` | **`85,802`** |
> | 8949 **loss** `(28,801)` | **`(28,802)`** |
> | **2026 carryforward** `28,801` | **`28,802`** |
> | **§4 decision 3's ALTERNATIVE branch** — line 10 `68,301` · loss `(11,301)` | **`68,302`** · **`(11,302)`** — ⚠️ **added 2026-09-11 by the second review: the banner said "in full" and left this branch out, and §5's own row was internally off by one** *(68,301 beside a loss of 11,302)* |
>
> ✅ **§3P ②A and ②C are the as-keyed authority; this section stays as the derivation.** ⛔ **Do not
> re-key line 2 to chase the dollar.** ⚠️ **The same ±1 sits unbannered in §3F's comparison table, §3L ③
> and §3M ② — read those as history, not as figures to type.** ✅ **§6 item 22 was a LIVE item carrying
> the old figures and has been updated in place instead.**

#### The basis roll-forward 🗺️ *Form 7203 (Rev. 12-2022), **page 1**, Part I*

| Line | ⌨️/ƒ | Concept | Value | **Where it came from** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A / B** | ⌨️ | S corporation | `GOSSIP MIAMI LLC` · EIN `99-2586917` | |
