> ✅ **NO CAPITAL GAIN ON THE DISTRIBUTION, by a wide margin.** The form's own note under line 6:
> *"If line 6 is larger than line 5, subtract line 5 from line 6 and report the result as a capital
> gain on Form 8949 and Schedule D."* **Line 6 is 20,700 against a line 5 of 107,134.** ⛔ **The test
> is against her BASIS, not against the company's AAA** — the company-level pool decides
> presentation on the 1120-S and settles nothing here.
>
> 🛠️ **ENTRY ROUTE — and this one has bitten before.** 🔴 **In ATX 2025 Form 7203 Part I takes NO
> keyboard entry at all**: the form prints its own banner, **`Basis information is entered on K1
> Input (1120S)`** *(established at this firm, [`tax-return-sop`](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/)
> §4B item 8d)*. ✅ **Work the `K1 INPUT (1120S)` sheet and read Form 7203 as a RESULT.**
> ⚠️ The sheet's header text says: *"Scroll down to the `Basis Limitation` section on this tab."*
> ⛔ **If a line on Form 7203 refuses your typing, that is the software behaving correctly — do not
> hunt for a permission.**
>
> ### 📘 HOW FORM 7203 WORKS — written 2026-09-11 for someone who has not met it
>
> _(Lilian: **"es una forma con la cual no estoy familiarizada y me cuesta mucho trabajo entenderla:
> cómo funciona, cómo modificar los números ahí."** Kept here because the next preparer will ask the
> same thing, and because the answer to "how do I change the numbers" is **you don't — not on the
> form**.)_
>
> **① WHAT IT IS, IN ONE IDEA.** Form 7203 is a **running balance of what her shares have cost her for
> tax purposes.** Treat it as a bank account for the investment:
>
> | | Effect on the balance |
> |---|---|
> | Money she **put in** *(the purchase, later contributions)* | ⬆️ **up** |
> | The company's **profit**, which she is taxed on whether or not she takes the cash | ⬆️ **up** |
> | Money she **took out** *(distributions)* | ⬇️ **down** |
> | **Non-deductible expenses** the company had | ⬇️ **down** |
> | **Losses** she actually deducts | ⬇️ **down** |
>
> 🔑 **The balance only matters for two things, and both are live on this return:**
> ① **She may only take out up to the balance.** Take out more and **the excess is a capital gain** —
> that is the form's own note under line 6, and it is exactly what is happening here.
> ② **When she sells, the balance is what comes off the sale price** to give the gain or loss.
>
> **② WHY NOTHING CAN BE TYPED ON THE FORM.** 🔴 **In ATX, Part I is COMPUTED, not typed.** The form
> carries its own banner — **`Basis information is entered on K1 Input (1120S)`** — so clicking on
> line 1 and typing does nothing. ⛔ **That is the software behaving correctly; it is not a locked
> field and there is no permission to hunt for.**
>
> **③ WHERE EACH NUMBER COMES FROM — three sources, and only one of them is automatic.**
>
> | Source | Lines | State on the 2026-09-11 draft |
> |---|---|---|
> | **The K-1, automatically** | **3a** income · **6** distributions · **8a** non-deductible | ✅ **already correct** — 19,515 · 20,700 · 633 |
> | **LAST YEAR's Form 7203** | **line 1** *(its line 15)* | 🔴 **EMPTY** — ⛔ **the software cannot know it; it is in the 2024 filed package** |
> | **The facts of this year** | **line 2** contributions · **line 13** the disposal · **box D** how the stock was acquired | 🔴 **EMPTY** |
>
> 🔑 **That is the whole problem in one sentence: ATX filled in everything the K-1 carries and left blank
> the three things the K-1 has no box for.**
>
> ⛔ 🛑 **AND THE REASON IS NOT "THE COMPANY RETURN DOES NOT HAVE THE NUMBER" — that is FALSE, and this
> paper said it** *(verification workflow, 2026-09-11, all three refuters)*. **ATX's own 1120-S
> shareholder input carries a `Stock and Loan Basis` grid whose FIRST column is `Stock Basis`, and
> [Gossip §6I](../gossip-miami-llc/2025-form-1120s.md) instructs keying her 86,609 into it** — grid
> line 1 lines up with Form 7203 line 1 and grid line 2 with line 2. **The figure IS in the company
> file.**
> 🔑 **The real reason is that tax software DELIBERATELY does not export basis from the entity return
> to the 1040:** the entity-side figure is an estimate built from corporate records, and importing it
> would override the limitation and carryover tracking the 1040 program does. **Outside basis is
> figured on the 1040 by design.** ⚠️ **Well-sourced from vendor knowledge bases, NOT verified on ATX
> specifically** — `files.cchsfs.com` returns 403 through this environment's proxy *(FOLLOW-UPS row
> 98)*. ⛔ **Do not upgrade this to a verified fact.**
>
> ⚠️ 🔵 **AND "IT MUST BE KEYED" IS NOT THE GENERAL RULE — it is true of THIS return only.** The
> banner's own words are *"to track basis **from year to year**"*: 🔑 **where the firm prepared the
> prior-year 1040 in ATX, line 1 PROFORMAS FORWARD automatically with no keystroke.** ⛔ **It has to be
> typed here because 2024 was prepared by another firm**, so there is nothing to roll forward from.
>
> ⓘ **What the K-1 legitimately carries, verified at source on the 2025 forms:** box 1 → line 3a ·
> box 16C → line 8a · box 16D → line 6 · box 16 A/B → line 3k · box 15C → line 3j. ⛔ **Part II of the
> K-1 has NO capital-account item** *(unlike item L on a Form 1065 K-1)*, **box 17's full code list
> A–BD contains no stock-basis code** *(17D `Basis of energy property` is a naming trap — it feeds
> Form 3468)*, **and the Instructions for Form 1120-S never mention Form 7203 at all.** 🔒 *"You are
> responsible for keeping the information needed to figure the basis of your stock in the
> corporation"* — the identical sentence appears in both the K-1 shareholder instructions and the
> Form 7203 instructions.
>
> **④ AND HERE IS WHAT THE EMPTY LINE 1 IS DOING.** With line 1 blank, line 5 is only **19,515** while
> line 6 is **20,700** — so the form thinks she took out **more than she had**, and its note fires:
> 🧮 **20,700 − 19,515 = 1,185**, reported as a capital gain. ⛔ **That 1,185 on Schedule D and on
> 1040 line 7a is not a real gain. It is the symptom of the empty line.**
>
> **⑤ WHAT TO TYPE, AND WHERE.** 🛠️ Open **`K1 INPUT (1120S)`** for Gossip Miami and scroll to the
> **`Basis Limitation`** section *(the sheet's own header says so)*:
>
> | | | |
> |---|---|---|
> | **line 1** | **86,609** | 📖 her **2024** Form 7203 line 15, in the filed package |
> | **line 2** | **1,010** ⚠️ *(or 1,011 — see below)* | 🔴 **NOT a fact only she knows — it is a COMPANY LEDGER fact:** her half of the S-period `Owner Contribution` pool *(Gossip §2E ⑥)*. **It is on no K-1 box, which is why no import carries it — but the reason is "no box", not "the corporation does not know".** ⚠️ **And it is still an OPEN 1120-S item** *(Gossip §6 finding 10 — the pool is split 50/50 by assumption)*. 🧮 **2,021.45 ÷ 2 = 1,010.725, which ROUNDS TO 1,011; this paper carries 1,010, a truncation.** ⓘ **Worth 1 on line 5, line 10 and the 8949 basis — so 1 on the capital-loss carryforward and nothing on the refund.** 🛠️ **Settle it when the Gossip split is settled.** |
> | **line 13** | **85,801** | the disposal itself |
> | **box D** | **(2) Purchased** | she bought in during 2024 |
>
> ✅ **Then read the FORM back — it recomputes itself:** line 5 **107,134** · line 7 **86,434** ·
> line 10 **85,801** · line 15 **0** · and 🔑 **the 1,185 is gone from Schedule D.**
>
> **⑤b 🖥️ WHAT THE SHEET ACTUALLY LOOKS LIKE — from Lilian's screenshot, 2026-09-11.** ✅ **The route is
> confirmed in the software's own banner** *(quoted in §9)*. **What the screen shows, so nobody hunts
> again:**
> · The sheet opens on an **`Input`** tab. **The K-1 boxes are what you see** — section 1 *(ordinary
> income 19,515, correct)*, then 10, 11, 12, 13. ⛔ **The `Basis Limitation` section is NOT among
> them: it is FURTHER DOWN the same tab**, past section 13. 🟠 **The sheet's own red note under
> section 11 SUPPORTS it — it is about §179 carryover amounts, so it shows there IS more below rather
> than proving where the section sits — *"Enter Basis/At-Risk Sec 179 carryover amounts in the appropriate section
> BELOW."***
> · **The bottom tabs are `Pages & Worksheets · Input · Detail · Shareholder Debt Basis · Allowed
> Amounts and Carryover`.** ⛔ **None of them is where beginning stock basis goes** — `Shareholder
> Debt Basis` is Form 7203 **Part II** *(all zero here)* and `Allowed Amounts and Carryover` is
> **Part III**.
> · **`7203, Basis Wkst` is a form in the left sidebar** — 🔑 **that is where to READ the result back,
> not where to type it.**
> · 🆕 **A step this paper did not have: the banner says *"Mark all K1 1120S records to calculate basis
> limitation."*** ⚠️ **If the section is filled and Form 7203 still prints empty, that marking is the
> next thing to look for.**
>
> 🔴 **AND THE SCREENSHOT SHOWS A DEFECT THAT IS NOT ABOUT BASIS: `Final K-1` IS UNCHECKED.**
> ⛔ **The Gossip return issues her K-1 with `Final K-1` CHECKED** — established on that side and
> confirmed 2026-08-27 *(Gossip §4 decision 10: she disposed of her entire interest and receives no
> further K-1)*. 🛠️ **Tick it on this sheet so the 1040 agrees with the K-1 the company files.**
> ⓘ *`Complete disposition of a passive activity` stays unticked — Gossip is nonpassive.*
>
> **⑤c ⚠️ "DO NOT RE-IMPORT" WAS TOO STRONG — corrected 2026-09-11.** ⛔ **Re-importing the K-1 cannot
> supply lines 1, 2 or 13.** ✅ **But THREE of Part I's lines DO come off the Gossip return through the
> K-1 — 3a (box 1), 6 (box 16D) and 8a (box 16C) — and line 10, the 85,801 the Form 8949 runs off, is
> a pure function of those three plus lines 1 and 2.** 🔴 **So the re-import §6 item 19 already mandates
> after the Gossip 1120-S is filed and accepted is NOT optional**, and **six of that return's nine live
> items still move box 1 or box 16D** *(Gossip §8D names the pooled `Draw` split as moving "each
> shareholder's Form 7203")*.
> 🛠️ **After that re-import: read Form 7203 back to line 15, and re-read the 8949's cost basis off
> LINE 10.** ⚠️ **Whether a re-import clears the hand-keyed `Basis Limitation` entries is NOT
> established and nobody here has ever run one** — 🔑 **the design reason above suggests it does not,
> since basis is excluded from the export, but that is an inference.** 🛠️ **Note the four figures
> before re-importing so they can be re-keyed if they vanish.**
>
> **⑥ 🛟 IF IT WILL NOT LET YOU KEY LINE 13 — DO NOT FIGHT IT.** 🔑 **The number that matters is
> LINE 10**, because line 10 is the basis the Form 8949 runs off. **Whether line 15 ends at 0 or at
> 85,801 changes nothing on this return.** ⚠️ **The entry route for line 13 specifically has never
> been watched working** *(§3H ④ — the vendor's help is unreachable from this environment)*. **Lines
> 1 and 2 are the ones that must land.**
>
> ⓘ **ATX also prints an `AMT Form 7203` copy** *(pages 32–33 of the SECOND print, 37 pages)* **with the same
> blanks.** 🔑 **It is ATX's own AMT tracking worksheet, not an IRS form that gets filed** — **look at
> it after the fix**, but do not key it separately unless it stays wrong.

> ### 🔵 "THE ENDING BASIS SHOWS ZERO" — asked by Lilian, 2026-09-11, and ZERO IS THE RIGHT ANSWER
>
> 🔑 **Line 15 asks what she owned at the END of the corporation's tax year. She sold 100% on 30
> October, so she owned nothing.** ⛔ **Do not fight the form and do not type over it.**
> ✅ **The 85,801 is not lost — it is LINE 10**, and line 10 is what the Form 8949 runs off.
>
> 🛠️ **BUT THERE ARE TWO KINDS OF ZERO, AND ONE LOOK SEPARATES THEM — read LINE 1 and LINE 10:**
>
> | What the form shows | What it means | What to do |
> |---|---|---|
> | ✅ **line 1 = 86,609 · line 10 = 85,801 · line 15 = 0** | **The form is RIGHT.** The disposal came off at line 13 | ⛔ **Nothing. Read 85,801 onto the 8949 from LINE 10** |
> | 🔴 **line 1 = 0** *(and everything else 0)* | **The form is EMPTY — nothing was ever keyed.** The `Basis Limitation` section on `K1 INPUT (1120S)` was never filled | 🛠️ **Fix §3L ④ DEFECT 2 FIRST** *(Gossip's Schedule E row must be `S`, not `P`, with column (e) ticked)*, then key the sheet from the table above |
>
> ⚠️ 🔒 **ONE THING THE INSTRUCTIONS SAY THAT DOES NOT APPLY HERE, recorded so nobody applies it:**
> *"If a **portion** of your stock is redeemed, sold, or otherwise disposed of during the year, attach
> **two separate forms**. Use the first to figure your stock basis at the date of sale and the second
> to figure your stock and debt basis at year end."* ⛔ **That is the PARTIAL case. Hers is a COMPLETE
> disposal**, so one form does both jobs: line 10 is the date-of-sale basis and line 15 is the year-end
> position, which is zero. 🟠 **The instructions give no explicit rule for a complete disposal** — there
> is no "final" box on the form — **so this is a reading, and it is the one the line's own words
> support.** *(Form 7203 and its instructions, Rev. 12-2022, read at source 2026-09-11.)*

#### 🔴 THE SALE — Form 8949 and Schedule D

| | Value | **Where it came from** |
|---|---|---|
| Description *(paste)* | `50% MEMBERSHIP INTEREST IN GOSSIP MIAMI LLC` | ⚠️ **the return reads `Gossip Miami LLC Stock`** — **both are accurate; keep ONE, do not key both.** §3Q ④A |
| Date acquired | **2024** *(April)* | ⚠️ **Column (b) needs an actual DATE, not a month** — read it off the 2024 share purchase agreement before keying. Gossip paper §8B |
| Date sold | **10/30/2025** | 📖 Double note 485291 — transfer effective 12:01 a.m. |
| **Gross proceeds — her half** | **63,000** | 🧮 **= 126,000 × 50%.** 📖 the Membership Interest Purchase Agreement: 100% sold to a single purchaser for **$126,000**, paid in full at closing by wire |
| **Selling expense — her half of the broker fee** | **(6,000)** | 🧮 **= 12,000 × 50%.** 📖 same note: broker fee **$12,000**, *"payable by the sellers at closing"* |
| **Net proceeds** | **57,000** | 🧮 63,000 − 6,000 |
| **Cost or other basis** | **85,801** | ⓘ **§3O is now the single sale table — work from it.** ƒ ← 🔴 **Form 7203 line 10** — the basis **immediately before the disposal** — ⛔ **NOT line 15**, which is correctly **zero** because she owned nothing at year end *(corrected 2026-09-11)*. 🛑 **READ THIS FIGURE BACK OFF THE 8949 BEFORE FILING:** if anything in the software pulls the cost basis from line 15, it reads **0** and a **(28,801) LOSS becomes a 57,000 long-term GAIN.** 🔴 **That does not merely cost tax — it DESTROYS the EIC:** the investment-income limit is **11,950** *(Pub 596, §3J)*, so a 57,000 gain disqualifies them outright and **7,152 disappears**, with the ACTC and the Form 8962 percentage moving behind it |
| 🔴 **Gain / (LOSS)** | **( 28,801 )** | 🧮 57,000 − 85,801 |
| Holding period | 🔒 **LONG-TERM** | acquired in **April 2024**, sold **30 October 2025** — more than one year |
| Form 8949 | 🔴 **Part II, box F** | ⛔ **NOT box C — that is the SHORT-term one.** Part I is **A/B/C and G/H/I**, **Part II is D/E/F and J/K/L** *(the G–L boxes are the Form 1099-DA digital-asset ones)*, and F is *long-term, not reported to you on a Form 1099-B*. ✅ Schedule D line 10 proves it on its own face: *"Totals for all transactions reported on Form(s) 8949 with **Box F or Box L** checked."* An LLC-interest sale produces no 1099-B *(2025 forms, read at source 2026-09-10)* |
| Schedule D | line 10 → line 15 → **line 16 = (28,801)** → 🔑 **line 21 applies the cap** and is what reaches 1040 line 7a | |
| **1040 line 7a** | 🔴 **( 3,000 )** | 🔒 **§1211(b) caps the deduction against ordinary income at $3,000 on a joint return.** ⛔ **Not the whole 28,801** |
| **Carries to 2026** | 🔴 **28,801 — the FULL loss** | 🛑 **NOT 25,801.** Taxable income is (6,417), so none of the 3,000 is absorbed. ⚠️ **This figure tracks taxable income and has moved twice — see the box below** |

>
> ### 🛑 THE $3,000 IS DEDUCTED AND NOT CONSUMED — THE WHOLE **28,801** CARRIES FORWARD
>
> ⚠️ **This box has moved three times in two days, and the reason is worth keeping: it tracks TAXABLE
> INCOME, which the vehicle question kept moving.** On the client's own figures AGI was 26,859 and
> the answer was 28,801. **Standard mileage lifts AGI to 28,959 — enough to turn worksheet line 3
> POSITIVE, absorbing 459 and cutting the carryforward to 28,342** *(re-derived on today's bases —
> §3F's comparison table)*. ✅ **The actual-expense figures take AGI back down to 25,083, line 3 is
> negative again, and the whole loss carries.**
> 🔑 **The lesson is the mechanism, not the number: this figure is a FUNCTION of taxable income, so
> it must be re-derived whenever anything upstream moves — it is not a constant.**
>
> **The obvious arithmetic — 28,801 − 3,000 = 25,801 — is WRONG**, because **taxable income is
> negative**: AGI 25,083 against a 31,500 standard deduction is **(6,417)** (§3J). A capital loss is
> only *used* to the extent there is income to absorb it.
>
> 🔒 **The Capital Loss Carryover Worksheet settles it, and its trigger sentence describes this return
> exactly:** *"Use this worksheet … if (b) the amount on your Form 1040 … line 15 **would be less than
> zero if you could enter a negative amount on that line**."*
> ⚠️ **Read the citation precisely, because the worksheet's year is not the carryover's year.** What
> was read at source on 2026-09-10 is the worksheet printed in the **2025** Schedule D instructions —
> *"Capital Loss Carryover Worksheet — Lines 6 and 14"* — and **that one computes 2024 → 2025**, fed
> from the prior return. ⛔ **The 2025 → 2026 carryover is computed by the SAME worksheet as it will
> appear in the 2026 instructions, which do not exist yet.** ✅ **The mechanism is identical year to
> year and does not change** — which is why the lines are numbered below as the read worksheet numbers
> them — **but this paper has not read the instrument that will actually produce the 28,801.**
> 🔑 **ATX computes it automatically from this return; the check is to confirm its figure, not to type
> one.**
>
> | Worksheet line | | Here |
> |---|---|---|
> | **1** | taxable income, as a negative if it would be | **( 6,417 )** |
> | **2** | the loss deducted on Schedule D line 21, positive | **3,000** |
> | **3** | combine 1 and 2 — *"if zero or less, enter -0-"* | ✅ **-0-** — the loss is not absorbed |
> | **4** | the smaller of line 2 or line 3 | ✅ **-0-** |
> | **13** | 🔴 **LONG-TERM CARRYOVER TO 2026** = line 9 − line 12 | 🔴 **28,801 — the full loss** |
> ⓘ *Line numbers as they appear in the worksheet read on 2026-09-10; the 2026 instructions may renumber, and §3K's re-check should read them off the form in front of it.*
>
> ✅ **So the carryover to 2026 is 28,801** — **and on §4 decision 3's alternative it is 11,301.**
> ⚠️ **Both are one dollar low as keyed — `28,802` and `11,302`; §3I's banner covers both branches.**
> ⚠️ **Neither changes the 2025 tax by a cent**; both change what is available later. 🔑 **All 3,000
> is deducted on line 7a and none of it does any work**, against a standard deduction already bigger
> than the income.
>
> ⚠️ **THREE MORE THINGS TO SAY OUT LOUD ABOUT THIS LOSS.**
> **①** ⛔ **It is NOT a §1244 ordinary loss.** §1244 only reaches stock **issued by the corporation
> to the taxpayer**; she **bought** her interest from the prior owners in 2024. **The capital-loss
> cap applies.**
> **②** ⛔ **§751 "hot assets" does not arise.** That is a **partnership** rule; this is an **S
> corporation**, so the whole thing is capital. *(The agreement's line that the price is "allocated
> completely towards goodwill" is asset-sale language sitting inside an interest-sale document — the
> Gossip paper already records it as a drafting artefact, not as a tax position.)*
> 🟠 **AND CLOSE THAT PROPERLY RATHER THAN BY CROSS-REFERENCE** *(tax review, 2026-09-11)*. The reason a
> goodwill allocation cannot bite is that **no election turns this stock sale into an asset sale**, and
> the obvious one — **§338(h)(10)** — is unavailable because a **single individual purchaser is not a
> corporation.** ⛔ **But §336(e) IS available to S-corporation SHAREHOLDERS on a disposition of 80% or
> more**, which this is, and it would recharacterise the whole transaction. 🛠️ **One look settles it:
> confirm no §336(e) election statement exists in the closing package.** ✅ **Nothing suggests one does**
> — an election needs a written agreement between the seller and the corporation — **but "nothing
> suggests it" is not the same as "it was checked".**
> **③** 🔴 **The loss swings by 17,500 on §4 decision 3.** If the 2024 opening basis of **100,000**
> is corrected to what she actually paid (**82,500**), her **line 10** becomes **68,301** *(line 15 stays zero)* and the loss
> becomes **(11,301)**. ⚠️ **It changes 2025's tax by almost nothing — the 3,000 cap binds either
> way — but it changes the CARRYFORWARD by 17,500**, and that is real money in later years.

#### ✅ THE EIC — IT DOES NOT FALL. IT RISES TO THE 2025 MAXIMUM

> 🛑 **THIS SECTION SAID THE OPPOSITE UNTIL THE INDEPENDENT REVIEW, AND THE ERROR IS WORTH KEEPING
> ON THE PAGE.** The first version reasoned: *"2024's AGI was 9,724 and 2025's is far above it — nearly
> three times higher — and the credit phases out as income rises."* ⛔ **Every clause of that is true
> and the conclusion is still wrong**, because it assumed 2024's 6,960 sat somewhere on the rising
> part of the curve. **It did not: 6,960 was the 2024 MAXIMUM.** §9 had already listed the EIC tables
> as *"not verified"* — and the direction was asserted anyway. **That is the failure, not the
> arithmetic.**

🔒 **Read off the 2025 Publication 596 EIC Table, 2026-09-10.** Married filing jointly with **two**
qualifying children:

| Earned income | Credit |
|---|---|
| **17,850 → 30,500** | 🔵 **7,152 — flat. This is the maximum** |
| 30,500 → 30,550 | 7,140 — **the phase-out starts here** |
| 31,200 → 31,250 | 6,993 |
| **the credit reaches zero at** | **64,430** *(Pub 596's own stated limit for MFJ, two children)* |

> ⚠️ **Those are the TABLE's brackets, not the statute's thresholds, and the difference matters only
> if a figure lands near an edge — which §6 item 3 could still cause.** 🔒 **Pub 596 p.20, read at
> source:** the earned-income amount for two or more children is **$17,880**, so the table's first
> full-credit bracket *(17,850–17,900)* straddles it. 🧮 **And the top can be derived from the table's
> own steps rather than quoted:** the credit is flat through 30,450–30,500 and falls to 7,140 in
> 30,500–30,550; at the bracket midpoints that puts the **MFJ phase-out threshold at about 30,470**,
> *inside* the last full-credit bracket. 🔑 **ATX computes from the table, so 30,500 is what the
> software will behave like** — but a figure between 30,470 and 30,500 is on the statutory phase-out
> even though the table still shows the maximum.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Their earned income** | 🔵 **26,551** *(the Schedule 8812 figure — §3J above)* |
| **Their AGI** | **25,083** |
| 🔵 **THE CREDIT** | **7,152** — and **both** figures sit on the plateau, so the lesser-of test changes nothing |
| Against 2024 | **6,960** → 🔵 **+192, not "far below"** |

✅ **Two things this settles, and both are worth telling her before she looks at the software:**

- 🔑 **THE INVESTMENT-INCOME LIMIT IS NOT TRIPPED.** For 2025 it is **$11,950** *(Pub 596, read at
  source)*. The only investment item on this return is a capital **LOSS**, and *capital gain net
  income* cannot be negative — it is **zero**. ⛔ **A GAIN on the sale is what would have killed the
  EIC outright**, and it did not happen.
- ✅ **THE LEASE ANSWER GAVE THE HEADROOM BACK. Earned income is 26,551 against a plateau top of
  30,500 — 3,949 of room** *(**3,919** to the statutory ≈30,470 — the box under the plateau table has that distinction)*. *(It was 28,327 on the client's own figures and 30,427 on standard
  mileage — **seventy-three** dollars from the top of the TABLE's last full-credit bracket, and **forty-three** from the statutory threshold; actual expenses deducts more, so earned income fell. ⓘ *It read ninety-six and sixty-six until 2026-09-11: those belonged to an earlier standard-mileage figure of 30,404, not to the 30,427 beside them.*
  §3F.)*
  ⚠️ **Every dollar of deduction REMOVED from here adds a dollar of earned income**, and past 30,500
  the credit falls at roughly **21 cents on the dollar**.
  🔴 **One open item can still push it over on its own:** §6 item **3** — Marat's **6,936 of
  `Education`** — which if disallowed adds roughly 6,450 of earned income and lands them **above**
  30,500. §6 item **14** (tips) moves it the same way but by less.
  ⓘ **Going the other way is safe:** the credit stays at 7,152 all the way down to **17,850**.
  ⛔ **This is the reverse of what the first version of this section said** *(it claimed more
  deduction meant more credit — true only below 17,850, which is nowhere near)*.

> the acquisition date off the 2024 agreement, and the cost basis read off **Form 7203 line 10** rather
> than line 15. 🔑 **That was the hard part and it is done.**
> 🛑 **BUT "RIGHT" IS NOT "FINAL", AND THIS LINE SAID "every judgement call" UNTIL 2026-09-12.** ⛔ **The
> row rests on THREE questions nobody has answered, all of them already in §3O ④ — see ④E below.**

#### ② 📘 WHY 392 — the rule, in plain words, and then where the number is printed

> 🔑 **THE RULE, AND IT IS ONE SENTENCE: you can only deduct a partnership's loss up to your BASIS in
> it.** *(§704(d).)* The rest is not lost — it **waits** until there is basis to absorb it.
>
> **And "basis" is a specific list, not a feeling.** It is **what you put in** *(cash or property)*
> **PLUS your share of the partnership's DEBTS** *(§752)* **PLUS income allocated to you, MINUS what
> you took out and minus losses already used.**
>
> 🛑 **THIS PARAGRAPH SAID SOMETHING ELSE UNTIL 2026-09-12, AND THE CORRECTION MATTERS BECAUSE IT IS
> THE PARAGRAPH WRITTEN TO TEACH THE RULE.** It read *"up to what you have actually PUT AT RISK"*, with
> *"put in nothing, deduct nothing"* and *"only to the extent he is actually out of pocket."*
> ⛔ **"At risk" is §465 — a DIFFERENT and LATER test** *(step 2 of the stack in ③ below, and on this
> entity it is **undecided**)*. ⛔ **And "put in nothing, deduct nothing" is falsified sixteen lines
> below by this very partnership:** Marat put in nothing and still has **392** of basis, because a
> **share of the partnership's debt counts as basis** even though no money left his pocket.
> ✅ **The right sentence for a first-time reader is the one at the top: BASIS, and debt is part of it.**
> *(Caught by the independent review, 2026-09-12.)*
>
> **The reason the rule exists:** a partnership loss is not automatically money Marat lost. It is the
> *company's* loss, allocated to him on paper. The law lets him deduct it only to the extent he has
> something invested to absorb it — otherwise anyone could join a loss-making partnership with nothing
> at stake and take a deduction for it.

**So the question is never "how big is the loss?" It is "how much has he got in it?"** And on this
partnership the answer is on the K-1's own face:

> ⚠️ **READ THE TABLE WITH ITS COLUMN HEADINGS: only ONE of these four rows measures BASIS.** Item L is
> the **tax capital account** — what he put in and took out — and it **excludes the debt share**, which
> here is the whole of his basis. **That is exactly why item L ends at (20,699) while his basis ends at
> 0.** ⛔ **Never read the capital account as the basis** *(the table did not say so until 2026-09-12,
> and a first-time reader would have)*.

| Where | What it says | What it means |
|---|---|---|
| **Item L** — beginning capital account *(⛔ **capital account, NOT basis**)* | **blank / 0** | his capital account starts the year at nothing |
| **Item L** — *capital contributed during the year* *(⛔ **not basis**)* | 🔴 **BLANK** | 🔑 **he contributed NO money in 2025** |
| 🎯 **Item K1** — his share of partnership liabilities | **Recourse `392`** *(nonrecourse blank)* | 🎯 **THE ONLY BASIS ROW ON THIS TABLE.** 🔒 **§752 treats a partner's share of partnership debt as a contribution of money** — so it gives basis even though nothing left his pocket |
| **Item L** — ending capital account *(⛔ **not basis**)* | **( 20,699 )** | ⚠️ **NEGATIVE** — the loss is bigger than anything he ever put in, which is exactly the situation §704(d) is about |

🧮 **So his basis is `392`. The loss is `20,699`. Only `392` of it can come off.**

**And the partnership already did that arithmetic for him. It is printed on TWO worksheets in the
package:**

> **`Partner's Basis Worksheet, Page 1`** — the running tally:
> ```
> Beginning of year                        0
> Capital contributions: Cash              (blank)
> Change in liabilities:  Current year   392
> Subtotal                               392
> Deductible losses: Ordinary business loss   392
> End of year                              0
> ```
>
> **`Partner's Basis Worksheet, Page 2`** — 🎯 **and this one uses the words themselves.** Its columns
> are **`Amount · Current Year Total Loss · Percent · Allowed Loss · Disallowed Loss`**, and its one
> filled row reads:
> ```
> Ordinary business loss   20,699   20,699   100.00   392   20,307
> Total losses and deductions        20,699   20,699           392   20,307
> ```

🔑 **`392` sits under `Allowed Loss`. `20,307` sits under `Disallowed Loss`.** ⛔ **The return currently
deducts the whole `20,699` — a figure that appears on that worksheet only in the "total loss" column,
never in the allowed one.**

#### ③ 🛑 AND THERE IS A SECOND LIMIT, WHICH TAKES THE 392 AWAY TOO

> ⚠️ **So the answer to her question is actually `0`, not `392` — and the 392 is the step in between,
> not the destination.** **§3H-1 ① has carried this since 2026-09-10; it is restated here because the
> question was asked about the 392 specifically.**

**Item G on the K-1 face is ticked `Limited partner or other LLC member`** *(verified again
2026-09-12 — the `X` sits immediately before "Limited", not before "General")*. A limited partner is
presumed **not to materially participate** under **§469(h)(2)**, which makes the loss **PASSIVE**.

> 🛑 **AND THIS IS A POSITION THE FIRM TAKES, NOT SETTLED LAW — §3H-1 ① says so and this section
> dropped the caveat until 2026-09-12.** ⚠️ **Read the checkbox's own wording — `Limited partner or
> other LLC member` is ONE box covering BOTH**, so the `X` distinguishes him from a *general* partner
> — **it does not establish that he is a "limited partner" for §469(h)(2).** 🔴 **PEP Hollywood IS an
> LLC**, and *Garnett* (132 T.C. 368), *Thompson* (87 Fed. Cl. 728) and *Newell* all hold that an LLC
> member with management rights is **not** a limited partner for that presumption and may use all seven
> material-participation tests.
> ✅ **§3H-1 ① follows the presumption anyway, and says exactly why: *"because only $392 is at stake —
> ⛔ not because the tick settles it."*** 🔑 **That justification has a dependency, and §6 item 38 is
> it** — the 392 ceiling rests on the basis worksheet **opening at zero**, and item 38 puts that
> opening in doubt *(Marat held 35% through 2024, and the 2024 return shows no PEP at all)*.
> ⛔ **So the two open questions must be read TOGETHER, not separately: if the opening basis is not
> zero, more than 392 survives §704(d) and the *Garnett* question stops being worth 392 and starts
> being worth thousands.** 🔴 **A passive
loss can only be deducted against passive INCOME, and this return has none** — Gossip Miami's 19,515
is *nonpassive* and cannot absorb it.

| The stack, in the order the law applies it | |
|---|---|
| **1 · §704(d) — basis** | 20,699 → **392 allowed**, 20,307 suspended |
| **2 · §465 — at risk** | 🟠 **UNDECIDED, and this paper refuses to default it either way** *(§3H-1 ① and §10 row 1)*. ⛔ **It said "probably at risk" until 2026-09-12** — a recourse share is the textbook at-risk amount, **but on an LLC a member is not personally liable for the LLC's debts, so a §752 RECOURSE share usually arises FROM a guarantee or a deficit-restoration obligation** — the branch that **does** require the Schedule E column (f) tick and Form 6198 *(Reg. §1.465-6(d) — ⚠️ `unverified`: the §465 regulations were issued as **PROPOSED** in 1979 and this paper has not established that §1.465-6 was ever finalised. **Check the citation before it leaves the firm**)*. 🔑 **On this entity the recourse label is a HINT toward a guarantee, not away from one.** **§6 item 12.** ⚠️ **And it is not cosmetic: if the 392 is NOT at risk it suspends at §465 and never reaches Form 8582 at all** — ④A's read-back and §7's split would both be wrong, and the whole 20,699 would be one carryforward rather than two |
| **3 · §469 — passive** | the surviving **392** goes to **Form 8582** and finds no passive income → **0 deducted** |

✅ **NET IN 2025: ZERO.** ⛔ **And nothing is lost — it is PARKED:**
**20,307** waits for basis *(he puts money in, or the partnership allocates him income or more debt)*;
**392** waits for passive income **or for a fully taxable disposal of his whole interest, which frees
it under §469(g)** *(§7's own row already said so)*. **Both follow him into 2026.**

> ⚠️ **"NOTHING IS LOST" IS TOO STRONG FOR THE §704(d) PIECE, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS NOT ACADEMIC FOR
> THIS HOUSEHOLD** *(review, 2026-09-12)*. **They are not symmetrical:**
> 🔵 **The §469 piece (392) is genuinely safe** — it is released against future passive income, **or in
> full on a fully taxable disposition of the entire interest** *(§469(g))*.
> 🔴 **The §704(d) piece (20,307) is NOT.** A basis-suspended loss is **personal to the partner and is
> lost if he disposes of the interest before basis is restored** — the established authority is
> ***Sennett v. Commissioner*, 80 T.C. 825, aff'd 752 F.2d 428 (9th Cir. 1985)**; the investment is
> instead reflected in the gain or loss on the disposition itself.
> ⚠️ **`unverified` — this paper has NOT read *Sennett* at source**, and by §9's own standard that
> stamp stays until it does.
> 🔑 **Why it matters here and not in the abstract: this household disposed of a DIFFERENT entity in
> this very year.** ⛔ **So the correct sentence is *"nothing is lost WHILE HE STAYS A PARTNER"* — and
> if PEP is ever sold or wound up, the 20,307 needs looking at before anyone assumes it survives.**

> ⚠️ **ONE HONEST CAVEAT, AND IT IS THE WORKSHEET'S OWN.** Its footnote reads: *"This worksheet was
> prepared based on partnership records. Please consult with your tax advisor for adjustments."*
> 🔑 **If Marat put money into PEP that the partnership did not record, his basis is higher and more of
> the loss is deductible.** 🛠️ **That is §6 item 12 and it is a question for the client** — ⛔ **not
> something to assume in either direction.**

The draft reads: Gossip `19,515` in (k) · Development Strategies `18,220` in (i) · **PEP `20,699` in

year's instructions computes the carryover INTO that year, from the year before.** ⛔ **A quote was

> 🔒 **The three-checkbox layout is the 2025 form's own.** *Instructions for Form 1065 (2025)*, **p. 57**,
> lays Statement A out with **`PTP` · `Aggregated` · `SSTB` per trade-or-business column**; the note
> *"SSTBs and PTPs can't be aggregated with any other trade or business"* is on **p. 56** — ⚠️ **two
> different pages, and this paper cited both to p.57 until the review on 2026-09-11.** ✅ **So three
> blank boxes beside a filled-in activity description is a positive answer, not missing information.**
>
> 🔴 **BUT THE (20,699) DOES NOT GO ON FORM 8995 THIS YEAR, AND THAT IS NOT A JUDGEMENT CALL.**
> 🔒 ***Instructions for Form 8995 (2025)*, p. 3, verbatim:** *"Losses or deductions from a qualified
> trade or business that are **suspended by other provisions of the Internal Revenue Code are not
> qualified losses or deductions and, therefore, are not included in your QBI for the year.** Such Code
> provisions include, but aren't limited to, sections 163(j), 179, 461(l), **465, 469, 704(d)**, and
> 1366(d). Instead, qualified losses and deductions are taken into account **in the tax year they're
> included in calculating your taxable income.**"*
> 🔒 **And the entry rule, same instructions, Line 1(c):** *"Do not include here any losses or
> deductions **suspended from use in calculating taxable income in the current year**."*
> ✅ **So: the activity IS qualified; its loss is suspended; it enters QBI in the year it is released.**
>
> 🔑 **WHICH IS THE REAL REASON THE ANSWERS MATTER, AND IT IS NOT THIS YEAR'S DEDUCTION.**
> 🔒 **Same instructions:** *"Losses and deductions **retain their status as either qualified or
> non-qualified from year to year while suspended.** Therefore, **you must track each category of loss
> or deduction until the loss or deduction is no longer suspended**"*, and when released *"the qualified
> portion … is treated as a **qualified net loss carryforward from a separate trade or business** …
> **See Line 3**."*
> 🛑 **2025 is the year of incurrence. The character is fixed NOW and travels with the 20,699.** ⛔ **And
> it comes back on LINE 3 — where it REDUCES a future deduction — not on line 16.** ✅ **§7 now carries
> it.**

##### ✅✅ CLOSED AT THE KEYBOARD 2026-09-11 — *"borré la 4 y la 5, ahora sí calculó el EIC."*

🟢 **The fix worked on the first try and the credit is in the return.** ⛔ **No override was needed and
no diagnostic re-fired** — clearing questions 4 and 5 was the whole of it. 🔑 **So ATX demanding an
answer on Step 3 line 5 was downstream of question 4 being answered**, exactly as predicted; with both
blank the demand goes away. ✅ **Worth ~7,152.** **The reasoning is kept below because it will recur** — ⚠️ **stated carefully,
because n = 1 and nothing establishes how question 4 came to be answered in the first place:**
🔑 **IF questions 4 or 5 get answered on a joint return, ATX demands the other, and a truthful `No` on
5 stops the credit.** ⛔ **Not "this happens on every joint return."**

##### 🔵 WHY IT HAPPENED — QUESTIONS 4 AND 5 OF STEP 3 ARE THE MARRIED-FILING-SEPARATELY TEST, AND THEY MUST BE LEFT BLANK

🔑 **Lilian ran `Check Return`, got *"Yes or No must be checked for Step 3, line 5"*, answered it `No`,
and the form replied *"STOP. You cannot take the credit."* She asked why.** ⛔ **Because question 5
should never have been reached.**

🔒 **The EIC Step 3 routing, read at source from the Instructions for Form 1040 (2025), page 41:**

| | The question | The routing |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *"Do you have at least one child who meets the conditions to be your qualifying child…?"* | **Yes → Continue** |
| 🔴 **2** | *"**Are you filing a joint return for 2025?**"* | 🔑 **"Yes. **Skip questions 3 through 6 and Step 4; go to Step 5.**"** |
| **3** | *"Are you a married taxpayer whose filing status is married filing separately or head of household?"* | **"No. Skip questions 4 and 5; go to question 6."** |
| **4** | *"Did you and your spouse have the same principal residence for the last 6 months of 2025?"* | Yes → Continue · No → skip 5, go to 6 |
| **5** | *"Are you legally separated…and you lived apart from your spouse at the end of 2025?"* | Yes → Continue · ⛔ **"No. STOP. You can't take the credit."** |

🛑 **QUESTIONS 4 AND 5 ARE THE §32(d)(2)(C) TEST FOR A SEPARATED SPOUSE.** Since ARPA a **married-
filing-separately** taxpayer can claim the EIC — **but only** if they did **NOT** share a principal
residence for the last six months *(**Q4 = No**)* **or** are legally separated under a decree and living
apart at year end *(**Q5 = Yes**)*.
⛔ 🔑 **READ THE POLARITY OFF THE TABLE ABOVE, NOT OFF THIS SENTENCE — an earlier version of it had Q4
backwards** *(tax review, 2026-09-11)*. **Q4 asks whether they had the SAME residence, so the answer
that HELPS an MFS filer is No.** **Q5 is reached only when Q4 is Yes**, so 🔴 **the STOP fires on
Yes-then-No**, not on "both No". ⓘ **And it is not only MFS: Q3 routes a married HEAD OF HOUSEHOLD
filer into 4 and 5 too.**
✅ **On a JOINT return the IRS skips the whole block twice over** — at its own question 2 *(joint →
skip 3 through 6)* and again at question 3 *(not MFS/HOH → skip 4 and 5)*.

⚠️ 🖥️ **ATX RENDERS IT DIFFERENTLY AND THAT IS WHERE THE TRAP IS.** Its list is **1** *"Did a child
live with you in 2025?"* · **2** *"Do you have at least one child who meets the conditions…"* · **3**
the MFS/HOH question · **4** · **5** · **6**. ⚠️ **The IRS's question 2 — "Are you filing a joint
return?" — is NOT VISIBLE ON THE SCREENSHOT Lilian sent** *(⛔ **which is not the same as "not on ATX's
screen"; the image may be cropped — `method.md` rule 1b**)*. ✅ **What IS pinned is the mapping:** ATX's
question 3 carries the IRS's question 3 wording **and its exact routing — "No. Go to Question 6."** —
**so question 3's `No` is what routes a joint filer past 4 and 5.**

🛠️ **THE FIX: CLEAR questions 4 and 5 — leave BOTH blank.** Leave 1, 2, 3 and 6 answered as they are
*(Yes · Yes · No · No)*. ⛔ **Do NOT answer 5 `Yes` to escape the STOP — that would be a false
statement on a signed return.** ⚠️ **If ATX re-raises the "Yes or No must be checked for Step 3,
line 5" diagnostic once both are blank, the DIAGNOSTIC is wrong on a joint return and the IRS routing
above is the authority** — record what it does, because nobody here has seen that state.
ⓘ **Likely mechanism: answering question 4 is what makes ATX demand question 5. Clear 4 FIRST.**

🔵 **AND THE SCREEN ANSWERS §6 ITEM 36's EIC HALF.** Step 1 question 2 — *"Do you, and your spouse if
filing a joint return, have a social security number issued on or before the due date…that allows you
to work and is valid for EIC purposes?"* — is answered **`X Yes`**, and Step 1 question 4 *(nonresident
alien for any part of 2025)* is **`X No`**. 🔑 **So the §32(m) hypothesis this paper raised as the best
explanation for the missing EIC is NOT the cause.** ⚠️ **It is a preparer's answer on a checklist, not
a verified fact — item 36 stays open for the PTC half — but it is no longer the EIC suspect.**

✅ **AND THE PRINT COULD NOT SETTLE IT — `Check Return` DID.** ⛔ **ATX prints no EIC worksheet and no
diagnostic page**, so the PDF had nothing further to give; 🔑 **the toolbar's `Check Return` named it in
one click.** **Worth remembering as the route, not just this once.**
⛔ **And the §32(m) hypothesis this section carried is DEAD** — the EIC screen answers it `Yes`. **It
was a good fit for the signature and it was wrong.**

🛑 **THE TWO RETURNS ARE ON TWO DIFFERENT METHODS FOR THE SAME CAR.** Reverse-engineered from the

| **Yuliana** | **3,275** | ✅ 6,282 × 16.67% = **1,047** — actual expenses, on the right base — **+ 2,228 of parking** | **1,286** *(7,716 × 16.67%, no advance)* |

> ✅ **HOW SOLID IS THAT? — because it is an inference from two printed totals, and the whole section
> leans on it.** Both were tested against **every combination** of the figures that could plausibly build
> them: the two fuel lines, repairs, insurance, the lease at both the books' 7,716 and the contract's
> 7,726.68, the amortised advance, each parking figure and the pool, at 100% and at each business
> percentage, plus the standard mileage rate. 🔑 **Each total has EXACTLY ONE solution**, and both are
> exact to the dollar with no rounding slack:
> **Marat 6,103 = standard mileage + the parking pool, and nothing else reaches it**;
> **Yuliana 3,275 = (1,583 + 456 + 775 + 3,468) × 16.67% + the parking pool at 100%, and nothing else
> reaches it.** ✅ **Her 20a of 1,286 identifies the BOOKS' 7,716**, because the contract's
> 7,726.68 × 16.67% is **1,288**. ⛔ **This line said the figure did not discriminate; it does** *(coherence
> review, 2026-09-11)*. ✅ **It proves both — the books' lease figure, AND that no advance was amortised.**

🛑 **THIS IS EXACTLY FAILURE MODE ② FROM §3F'S ENTRY ROUTE.** Once the lease is taken out of the
worksheet, the worksheet compares a lease-less Actual *(3,140)* against the full mileage rate *(3,875)*
and declares **Standard** the winner on Marat's return. ✅ **It is wrong**, because the lease it can no
longer see is worth another **3,863** — and 🔒 **Pub 463 bars the rate on this car anyway**, since a
leased car may use the rate only if it is used *"for the entire lease period"*.
⚠️ **It has not yet produced the worst version** — Marat's line 20a is blank, so the return is not
claiming the rate **and** the lease at the same time. ⛔ **But typing 3,863 on his line 20a without
first forcing `Actual` would do exactly that.**

🔴 **AND THE PARKING IS COUNTED TWICE.** **2,228 = Marat's 1,986 + Yuliana's 242, and it appears in
FULL on BOTH returns** — **4,456 claimed against 2,228 actually spent.** ⛔ **Pooling them was the
intention; taking the pool at 100% on each return is not, and it is the kind of error nobody sees
because each return looks internally consistent.** ✅ **§4 decision 14: each keeps their own, at 100%.**

#### ⑧ 🛠️ THE ORDER TO WORK IN — because three of these feed each other

1. **Dependents block** ② — everything refundable hangs off it.
2. **Schedule E** ④ — it sets AGI, and AGI sets the 8962 percentage and the credits.
3. **The vehicle** ⑤ and **the home office** ⑥ — they set the Schedule Cs, which set SE tax and earned income.
4. **Schedule D / 8949 / 7203** ③d–e.
5. 🔴 **Form 8962** ③a — **LAST of the figure work, not third.**
   ⚠️ **This list put it at step 3 *"because it needs the AGI from step 2"* until the tax review on
   2026-09-11, and that reason was wrong on its own list:** the vehicle and the home office move AGI
   **after** Schedule E, so an 8962 keyed at step 3 is computed on an AGI that is about to change.
   ⚠️ **The size, stated correctly:** those steps move **5,493 of DEDUCTION**, and AGI by **5,105** —
   the difference is the **388** rise in the deductible half of self-employment tax, which moves the
   other way. ⓘ *And the 5,493 is not purely vehicle and home office: one dollar of it is Marat's
   meals.* ⛔ **This note said "move AGI by 5,493" until the tax review the same day — that is the
   deduction figure, not the AGI figure.** 🔑 **What it actually needs is the FINAL AGI**, which does not exist until step 4 is in.
   ⓘ **Keying the form early is harmless if the software recomputes it** — the trap is READING its
   percentage before the Schedule Cs are done.
6. ✅ **Then tie out against §3K**: AGI **25,083** ⚠️ **— the return reads `25,085` as keyed, and that
   is CORRECT, not a break: §3P ⑥ and §6 item 39 name both dollars. Accept 25,085 and do not hunt the
   2** · taxable income **0** · total tax **4,037** ·
   credits **10,552** · **refund 6,515**. 🔑 **Re-read Form 8962 line 5 at this point** — on these
   figures it is **80%** of the poverty line, and the whole §3G position turns on which side of 100%
   and 150% it lands *(§6 item 25)*.

|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | 🔴 **Yuliana's home-office costs are moved OUT of Schedule C and onto Form 8829 at their FULL annual amounts**, letting the form apply the 30% | **Session, 2026-09-10 — a DEFECT correction, not a preference** | Key her P&L as delivered. ⛔ **That deducts the apartment rent TWICE** — once as 5,124 inside Schedule C and again through the home-office worksheet. **There is no version of the return where both stand** |
| **2** | **Marat's meals are keyed at 50% — 3,123, not the P&L's 6,245** | Session, 2026-09-10 | Deduct the gross. ⛔ **§274(n) halves business meals**, and the Schedule C line is captioned *"Deductible meals"* |

No vamos a utilizar esa parte de los $2,500 pagados… **No vamos a complicar las cosas si no estamos seguros.**"* | Spread the 2,500 over the 36-month term and take **833.33** in 2025, as this paper did for a day. ⛔ **Rejected by her, and the reason is the right one: HALF of that position is verified and half is not.** 🔒 **Verified — Pub 463: *"You must spread any advance payments over the entire lease period."*** 🟠 **Not verified — that a capitalized cost reduction IS an "advance payment" for that sentence.** ⛔ **The lease document never says so**; it discloses a *capitalized cost reduction of 2,500* and a 36-month term, and nothing more. **The bridge was this paper's own reasoning** *(the reduction lowers the adjusted cap cost and therefore the base rent, so it buys down future rent)*, **and the rival reading this paper offered — a cost of entering the lease, deductible when paid, all in 2024 — 🛑 DOES NOT SURVIVE.** 🔒 **Established 2026-09-11, after she ruled, from Pub 334 ch. 2 and ch. 8 and Pub 538** *(the very sources §6 item 31 had listed as "not read")*: *"You can't deduct expenses in advance… This rule applies to **any expense** paid far enough in advance to, in effect, create an asset with a useful life extending substantially beyond the end of the current-tax year"*, and the 12-month rule reaches only benefits not extending beyond twelve months. ⛔ **A payment at the start of a 36-month lease fails that under ANY characterisation**, so rent-in-advance and lease-acquisition-cost give the same answer: **spread.** 🔴 **HER RULING WAS CONDITIONAL ON THE UNCERTAINTY — *"no vamos a complicar las cosas si no estamos seguros"* — AND THE UNCERTAINTY IS SMALLER THAN SHE WAS TOLD.** ⛔ **The return stays as she ruled until she says otherwise; this is put back to her at §6 item 31, not reasoned around.** ⚠️ **Costs 79 of refund in 2025**, and 🔴 **leaves the whole 2,500 unclaimed in every year, 2024's 555.56 included.** 🛠️ **§6 item 31 carries what would settle it — and it returns next year with 833.33 more behind it.** 🔑 **She asked the question that exposed it: *"¿dónde lo leíste, en el documento de renta o dónde?"*** |

      this individual client — ⛔ **which is not evidence either way** *(Double omits properties with
      no value)*. ⚠️ **BUT THE MONEY SIDE IS NOT WHAT IT LOOKS LIKE.** §6651(b)(1) computes the
      failure-to-file penalty on *the tax required to be shown on the return*, **reduced by payments
      and by any credit claimable on it** — total tax **4,037** against credits of **10,552**, so the
      **penalty base is zero and there is no failure-to-file or failure-to-pay penalty on these
      figures.** ⛔ **So this changes what the return IS, not what it costs.** 🔑 **It is still worth
      knowing** — a late return with a refund runs the §6511 three-year clock, and the answer belongs
      in the file. *(Owner: Lilian — one look at the 2025 tax project or the e-file record)*

      rest of your payment only over the period to which it applies.**"*

      advance, even if you pay them in advance. This rule applies to **any expense** paid far enough in
      advance to, in effect, create an asset with a useful life extending substantially beyond the end of
      the current-tax year."*
      🔒 **Pub 334 (2025) ch. 2:** *"You can deduct an expense you pay in advance only in the year to which
      it applies."* 🔒 **Pub 538:** the **12-month rule** reaches only benefits **not extending beyond 12
      months after they begin.**
      🔑 **A payment at the start of a 36-MONTH lease fails the 12-month rule whatever you call it**, so
      **rent paid in advance** and **a cost of entering the lease** give the SAME answer — **spread over
      the term.** ⛔ **The alternative this paper described to her — all 2,500 deductible in 2024 — has no
      support in any source now in hand.**
      🔴 **SO THE DECISION SHOULD GO BACK TO HER, and this is the item that does it.** Her ruling was
      expressly conditional — *"no vamos a complicar las cosas **si no estamos seguros**"* — and she was
      told the question was open both ways. ⛔ **It is not.** ⚠️ **What is still genuinely unsettled is
      narrower and worth saying plainly: the publications never define "advance payment" and never use
      the words *capitalized cost reduction*, so the CLASSIFICATION step is still a reading** — but every
      classification on the table now points the same way.
      🛠️ **Two things would still help if she wants more than this:** ② **ask the lessor what the 2,500
      was applied against** *(it should confirm it reduced the capitalized cost)*; ③ **look at how the
      2024 preparer treated it** *(2024 claimed nothing for the car, so it settles nothing — but it says
      whether the question was ever reached)*.
      ⚠️ **WHAT IT IS WORTH:** **833.33 of BASE and ≈556 of DEDUCTION in 2025 — 79 of refund**, roughly
      the same again in 2026, and **2,500 of base across the lease** *(≈1,667 of deduction at 66.66%)* —
      so this is a recurring decision, not a one-year rounding. ⛔ **Base and deduction are not the same
      number and this paper has slipped between them before; §5's priced row says both.**
      ⚠️ 🟠 **THE 2024 SHARE RESTS ON AN UNDECLARED MONTH CONVENTION — declared here now** *(tax review,
      2026-09-11)*. This paper uses **555.56 = 8/36**, counting **May 2024** (the month of signing) as a
      whole month. 🔒 **Pub 538's Example 1 allocates from the START DATE** *(a 1 July start gives 6/36
      in year one)*, which on a **29 May** signing gives **7 months → 486.11**, and moves 2027 from four
      months to five. **Both are self-consistent; neither is written anywhere but here.** 🛠️ **Pick one
      before any of this is claimed, and use the same one in 2026 and 2027.**
      ⛔ **AND TWO OVERSTATEMENTS IN "the whole 2,500 is unclaimed, 2024's share included", corrected:**
      ① **2024's deductible amount was its share × the 2024 BUSINESS-USE percentage**, which §6 item 28
      says is **unknown and may be zero** — so nothing was necessarily lost there; ② **2024 is FILED**,
      so recovering it needs an **amendment**, which is Lilian's call, not an automatic consequence.
      🟠 **AND THE ORIGINAL MIDDLE BUCKET IS STILL OPEN UNDERNEATH IT — and the SAME finding now reaches
      it** *(tax review, 2026-09-11)*: predelivery **995** + tag agency **199** + the **83.58** of tax on
      them = **1,277.58**, which as a lease acquisition cost would amortise the same way — **425.86 of
      base to 2025**, ≈ **284** of deduction, ≈ **40** of refund. 🔑 **The branch that would have put it
      all in 2024 is the SAME branch Pub 334 and Pub 538 have just foreclosed**, so if those sources
      settle the 2,500 they settle this too. ⛔ **Not taken, and §5 prices only the 2,500 — so the
      question Lilian is being asked is understated by 1,277.58 of base unless the two are put together.**
      ⓘ 🟠 **AND THE BETTER AUTHORITY FOR THIS HALF IS NOT A PREPAID-EXPENSE PARAGRAPH:** a cost of
      **acquiring a lease** is capitalised and amortised over the lease term under **§178** and
      **Reg. §1.162-11(a)**, with **Reg. §1.263(a)-4(d)(6)** *(amounts paid to create or enhance a
      leasehold)* and its 12-month rule at **§1.263(a)-4(f)**. ⛔ **STATED FROM KNOWLEDGE, NOT READ AT
      SOURCE** — eCFR is blocked by this environment's egress policy *(§9 records the same block on
      Regulation Z)*. 🛠️ **Verify before relying on it in a filed position.**
      ⛔ **Title 230 and FL fees 66.50 are NOT in this question** — Pub 463 makes them ordinary car
      expenses deductible **when paid**, so they belonged to **2024**. *(Lilian)*

### 🛑 WHAT THIS PAPER GOT WRONG AND FIXED — kept, because the corrections are worth more than the figures

⚠️ **Most corrections are recorded where they happened** *(§3F ③ the advance-payment base · §3F ④ the
FMV measure · §3J Form 8995 line 4, the QBI net/gross · §3J the SE-tax rounding · §3G the 150% crossing)*.
🆕 **And so are the 2026-09-11 batch** — ⓘ *§3K and §6 item 16 the **prior-year safe harbour** (2024's
tax is net of refundable credits, so it is zero — and the UNCONDITIONAL route is line 8, not the
Exception; the Exception's own authority is **Pub 505 ch. 2**, not Form 2210 line 8) · §3F and §4
decision 14 the **parking authority** (Pub 463 does not settle it on its face; it is a judgement, and
Form 2106 line 2 is not merely "the employee route") · §8 the **Form 8962 step ordering** (it was third
"because it needs the AGI from step 2", while steps after it moved AGI) · §3H and §10 the **at-risk
default** (recourse IS at-risk under §465(b)(2)(A), but on an LLC that cuts the other way and the
question is undecidable until §6 item 12) · §5 **833.33 of base is ≈556 of deduction**, not 833 · §3A
the **3,862/3,863 note**, which rested on the premise that line 20a computes when this paper's own
entry route TYPES it)*.
🆕 **And the 2026-09-11 NIGHT batch, all three caught by looking at the documents instead of re-reading
the paper** — ⓘ *§3H-1 ① the **22 / 23 over-read** (`More than one activity for at-risk purposes*` is
printed on the BLANK form; an unticked label was written up as an answer, and an inference about
splitting the loss by activity hung off it) · §6 item 13 the **"a negative QBI item carries forward"**
rationale (Form 8995 line 16 combines lines **2 and 3**, and line 2 is already the net — nothing
carries when total QBI is positive) · §9 the **"not an SSTB for both K-1s"** wording (Development
Strategies' K-1 face carries no §199A statement, so no SSTB answer exists to record — ⚠️ **and the
replacement wording must be *the FACE reports nothing and a page that could contradict it is missing
from our copy*, never *the partnership reported nothing***)*.
🆕 🛑 **AND THE INDEPENDENT REVIEW OF THE FOURTH-DRAFT COMMIT ITSELF, 2026-09-11 — it returned BLOCKING

🔑 **These four have no other home, and two of them were caught by a REVIEW rather than by the session
that made them** — which is the reason the review exists:

| | What was wrong | How it was caught | What it would have cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **1** | **The six-branch vehicle table this paper carried on 2026-09-10 was computed on the wrong base.** Every vehicle branch subtracted the new deduction from a Schedule C line 31 that **already carried the client's own vehicle figures** — double-counting the removal. | ⚙️ **This paper's own tie-out**, before anything reached a return | ⛔ **Every branch looked about 9,600 too generous, and the RANKING REVERSED**: standard mileage appeared to beat actual expenses by 1,029 when in fact it LOSES — **by 589 on today's figures** (§3F). 🔑 **A wrong ranking picks the wrong method, and the method binds the whole lease.** |
| 🔴 **2** | **The one-car proof was CIRCULAR.** Three "independent" pieces of evidence were offered and the decisive one — *11,075 total − 5,536 − 1,846 = 3,693 personal* — is **true by construction for any pair of figures**. The identical odometer was not evidence either: ⛔ **Marat filled in BOTH organizers.** | 🔍 **The independent review** | ⚠️ **Nothing on the return** — one car is still the working assumption, resting on the **3 : 1 P&L pattern** *(§3F ②)*. **But it was published as proof, and Schedule C line 46 is a line the taxpayer SIGNS.** §6 item 30 asks it properly. |
| 🟠 **3** | **The wrong box was named for the depreciation basis** — the retail instalment contract's **"Total Sale Price"**, which includes the whole finance charge. 🔒 **Pub 551: basis is the stated purchase price.** The right box is the **Cash Price**. | ⚙️ self-caught, verified in Pub 551 | ✅ **Moot now — it is a LEASE, so there is no basis and no depreciation.** ⚠️ **Kept because the next financed car will raise it again**, and an inflated basis is invisible once keyed. |
| 🟠 **4** | **A hash was over-claimed as closing a question it never touched.** The SHA-256 compared the **two copies of `33806941-Contract.pdf`** attached to the two organizers; it says nothing about **`IMG_7719.jpeg`**, which was never fetched. | 🔍 **The independent review** | ⚠️ **It would have closed §6 item 9 on evidence that does not exist.** The item stays open. |

🔑 **The pattern behind three of the four is the same: an inference was written down in the voice of a
finding.** ⛔ **Where this paper says PROVED, something was read or computed; where it says "the
working assumption", it was not** — and the distinction is the whole value of the document.
